# Three Crossroads Native: design decisions

## Borrowed World ground actors (source checkpoint)

Ground providers borrow one actor policy from the existing commander or World local-defender owner. Source owner -1 remains distinct from commander indices; an absent economy slot means the Source local actor's zero MP, Munitions and fuel, not a fabricated wallet. Game retains health, pose and alliances, while WorldRuntime retains local visibility and faction. The new lookup validates matching mode/runtime facts but does not admit a complete World tick. Native checks and the sole caller's phase admission remain separate.

The ground firing and damage adapters now resolve team claims, visible targets and alliances without indexing the local actor into Game.players. Projectile and Combat metadata remain their existing owners; all Environment calls still validate complete current membership and publish committed effects through the existing ordered sinks. Ordinary combat retirement keeps final removed objects for the captured World income list, rewards only actual enemy commanders and preserves retained shooter XP. Administrative territory elimination remains the separate accepted World provider and receives no ordinary death, bounty or hull callback.

World ability use follows the existing construction-mode price table. Command selection checks affordability without paying an aimed order early; an actual aimed launch pays only after its checked flight commits. Instant ability use pays the canonical commander Munitions wallet after its effect preparation. The local actor's zero resources can deny use without a new mutable wallet. This source checkpoint still requires final combined source review and native verification. It does not enable World logistics, aircraft, rail, construction, the sole tick caller, transport or client fields.

The captured recovery frame retains the original late list and production-building membership before later supply changes. World allies and local defenders use existing Game alliances. Local MP remains zero: eligible infantry advance their reinforcement timer without buying a model, while local vehicle repair cannot pay. Medic healing, mine discovery and the neutral team's signed bit 31 remain actual Source paths. Far medic travel uses the shared command route entrypoint and the normally joined World movement provider. The source-only merge retains the ground actor team/economy checks; native and whole-caller evidence remain separate.

The new maintained component recorders reproduce ten ground traces (49 actions, 59 snapshots) and six recovery traces (30 actions) from the pinned original constructor and actual phase helpers. Both preserve all 399 startup actors. The ground recorder also retains 616 structures with 3,012 sections, physical arrays and lossless actor overlays. Its native test starts a real World Match, then explicitly restores the recorded component boundary; it does not establish native constructor equivalence. Source checks and bounded runtime/recovery reviews pass. The native consumers have only been written and formatted, not compiled or executed. Actual World scheduling and whole-step replay remain owned by the sole tick lane.

## Final internal runtime source bundle (2026-10-13)

The final normal merge explicitly includes main `d1f2fb5`. That main tree is identical to already incorporated ordered-event head `a068dd8`; the merge adds ancestry without changing runtime source, approved header, original fixtures or the optimized test profile.

The separate normal merge combines Ground and construction `bf293cc` with RTI and Human `32670c2`. Ground retains its accepted stored Attack, Assist and Entrench Join anchors and the one real six-command queued Cover regression. Human retains detached owner-filtered observations and the three-lifetime receipt; RTI retains caller-owned scheduled batches, Current capture isolation and committed failure output. Shared routing composes the Ground provider with the two additive Human preview and path-history APIs. The trusted host keeps its read-only admission guard against the combined tick input rules. No source conflict requires a policy change.

Axel acknowledgment 032 confirms the original queue at main `d1f2fb5` and requires fresh checks on the exact final head for Linux, Windows and macOS, with the approved ABI 2 header unchanged. Envelope or host wiring requires interface review before merge. This source checkpoint does not satisfy those publication gates; combined native tests, including the new Cover regression, remain pending. Privacy, spectator and new gameplay decisions remain pending, and FPS and editor remain deferred.

## Trusted room and current-main source union (2026-10-13)

## Room-host output ownership regressions (2026-10-13, source checkpoint)

Strict preflight at `8a6f418` reached the complete companion unit JSON helper used by the ordinary effects consumer and exceeded that test crate's default macro expansion limit. The crate now uses the same explicit 256 limit as existing complete companion fixture consumers. No field is removed, and no production code or runtime stack limit changes.

Strict preflight at `02427d2` exposed two older fixture serializers accessing timeline fields before MatchSample became typed. The previously independently reviewed `456f64b` helper patch is applied unchanged: Default and Conquest constructors serialize Conquest fields in the original shape, while Territories explicitly refuses this ordinary fixture adapter. No runtime or fixture is changed.

Strict preflight at `23b11ca` then found the quiet engineering fixture reading the old report Environment field. The caller now exposes a borrowed filtered iterator over its single owned event sequence, so the same no-Environment assertion uses that accessor. A read-only report-consumer audit found no other stale field use. The change does not remove or weaken the assertion, alter the original fixture or mutate the report.

Strict all-target lint at `64cf6c5` reached all workspace packages and stopped at the new attachment admission method appearing after the snapshot test module. Moving that exact method body before the test module corrects the structural warning. Runtime logic and tests are byte-identical; the final native cycle remains pending.

The `c73096f` native continuation passes all seven Hub tests and three socket tests. Socket binds required the reviewed local-listener sandbox escalation; the denied attempt is kept separately. Snapshot checks pass 19 cases, then an older error expectation fails for a corrupt canonical strike owner. Direct support projection still refuses it as InvalidRecipient, while complete snapshot admission reaches the existing routing Rules guard first. Both boundaries are now asserted, with no runtime or fixture change. The final coherent cycle is still pending.

At `412c5df`, both real-event tests pass. The Hub group then stops six cases at the common Match constructor because disabled living weather requires the Map choice. The fixture now uses that canonical disabled-weather input, preserving the public admission guard and all lifecycle assertions. The earlier typed fixed-clear repair compiled but did not satisfy this runtime admission rule. Complete native checks remain pending.

The first `7b0f5a8` native attempt stopped at server test compilation: the weather choice requires `Fixed(WeatherKind::Clear)`, and per-player story tallies require `Some(slot)`. The corrections retain the intended clear weather and player ownership. The compiler also reports `AtomicU64::fetch_update` as deprecated in Rust 1.99. Its documented alias `try_update` retains the same relaxed orderings and checked identity increment. No tests passed in that failed attempt.

Two focused host tests exercise the real Match constructor, staged unit publication, ordinary rifle firing and registered building-section reconciliation. They do not insert expected events. An earlier rifle emits committed Flight events before a later mover exhausts its path identity. The host retains that typed failure prefix once and refuses another tick or capture, without consuming a future queued command. A successful section Hurt stays pending through Current captures, appears once in Scheduled output, and is discarded at an empty Scheduled boundary.

The tests and module include passed independent source review at the `853db25` integration checkpoint. They have not yet compiled or run. One frozen room-runtime tree will receive the affected native tests and full release, lint, formatting and FFI checks. Public match admission and complete ordinary-combat delivery remain separate work.

## Human policy source assembly (2026-10-13)

Compiled source `f011ce1a9feec6812c450ebd27eb5b11a6d1aadc` passes all seventeen focused human debug tests (ten library, two timing, two input and three state), all 280 locked workspace release tests with zero failures or ignored tests, strict all-target workspace Clippy, formatting and the locked release FFI build. This verifies the single-seat observer, detached human attention/state/input behavior and bounded quiet strategic callback. It does not establish host scheduling, all-seat delivery, issuer allocation, canonical actuator admission, broader strategic policies or full AI.

The following failure and repair notes describe earlier checkpoints in this proof. Their pending checks were completed by the final compiled-source gates above; the component scope limits remain.

Strict all-target Clippy then reaches the newly composed library tests and detects two registrations of the same fixture decoder file. A single cfg-test root module now supplies both state and policy assertions; the external integration target retains its own crate-local module. This changes test linkage only, without changing decoded values, assertions, production modules or the seventeen-test inventory. The diagnostic is preserved and final exact-head gates remain pending.

At `c7cf54a`, all seventeen focused human debug tests and 280 locked workspace release tests pass. Strict Clippy then finds one descending-sort style lint in idle camera group selection. The successor uses the same stable order with `Reverse(count)` as its key; equal-size groups retain first insertion order, with no RNG or selection change. The original comparator agrees with Source's descending group count. Debug and release binaries and the complete logs are copied outside the cache before rebuilding. Final exact-head checks remain pending.

The first focused human library run passes nine tests and fails one refusal assertion because its snapshot helper invokes the ordinary routing getter after deliberately making the context unsupported. The owner repairs only the test helper: it reads the existing canonical recipient rows and navigation buffer directly. No mode is remapped, and the runtime refusal guard remains unchanged. This exposes the intended before/after state for the real atomic-refusal assertion. The failed log is preserved; complete focused and final native gates remain pending.

The first native inventory compile found two missing generic packet annotations in no-view assertions and a borrowed-vector ownership error in fightResponse. The narrow successor copies the existing delivered-unit reference before the vector is consumed and names the test packet as `()`. It preserves victim selection, unit iteration, command and timing order. The failed command and compiler diagnostics are retained outside the cache; no human test had executed at that point.

The completed source union additionally consumes independently reviewed observer `8c0694b`. Its additive `commit_with_paths` preserves canonical warmed routing history across observation publication; no packet or callback API changes. The four observer native assertions, including the new history regression, remain uncompiled. Provider bodies match their owner freeze, and the complete human native cycle remains pending.

The separate callback assembly normally combines resumed state proof `4fd52ac` with observation checkpoint `96bcbdb`, including its checked bounded commander and ordinary Default ancestors. It preserves provider bodies and both documentation histories. This assembly is source work, not a native acceptance claim. Independent observation review found a prepared-routing publication cache-identity issue; that owner correction must be consumed and reviewed before native nomination. The state and observation leaves remain frozen.

`human_commander_policy` invokes the existing bounded quiet planner only through HumanState's due-plan callback. It rebuilds Observation from the camera-filtered detached packet, without reading Game or using the scripted difficulty beat. One seat-bound private SourceState and existing Ai-purpose MatchRandom stream accompany the separate human motor timing stream. The wrapper stages both private providers, collects typed policy commands in order, preserves their minimal original raw shapes and commits both only after complete human input processing succeeds. Unsupported output variants or extra command fields fail explicitly. The host binds the providers to the same seat and calls this leaf on its own staged values when later queue reservation must also succeed. All-seat observation publication, issuer sequencing, queue admission and completed-tick fault handling remain outside this leaf.

The maintained original full-step recorder delegates the unchanged Source thinker and preserves its actual undefined collector return. Easy, Normal and Hard plan at 124, 88 and 56, respectively; none is a scripted think beat. Three cases contain 296 original steps, seven delivered packets, three raw Move collections and six physical inputs. Complete packets are captured at every actual delivery, including tick zero, so the native replay never uses future economy or terrain facts in an earlier view. The focused test calls the actual native quiet policy with the three observed Source fractions at each real human planning boundary, checks raw commands and private aim state, then compares delayed input receipts. It does not replace the production purpose stream or claim native physical command acceptance. A separate production-stream twin and failed-projection assertion checks deterministic private publication and stream isolation. Both native assertions are written but uncompiled.

The constructor for human timing takes the recorded current Source seed at first activation; the strategic policy takes its independently recorded match seed. They represent different owners and purposes. Actual ground Attack/Retreat, wider strategic shopping/construction, other brains or modes, adaptive transport delivery, complete runner scheduling and whole human AI acceptance remain pending.
## Ordinary human commander timing (2026-10-13)

The resumed proof registers the three paused cap/locality/resumption and refusal assertions. The unchanged Source seam recorder now observes raw `think` attempts through an in-memory delegate that preserves callback returns; processed and resumed input rows are separately recorded. Five constructed contexts contain twelve helper calls. These protect motor scheduling and input processing, rather than claiming complete strategic policy or canonical admission.

Reset preserves the monotonically allocated identity of subscriber receipts while dropping camera, selection, timing, input history, suppression areas and batch cursors. A real original-source two-grenade replay resets after the first off-screen alert is answered at tick 63. The earlier subscriber object remains answered at 63; the second real hurt at 87 produces a distinct object, answered at 118. Its 159 full steps contain five delivered views and seven physical inputs, including an actual raw Retreat followed by Move. Native replay treats those commands as attempts; ordinary ground Retreat admission remains outside this component proof.

The Source reads current `g.seed` when a dormant commander first becomes active. It does not necessarily use the creation seed. All six earlier full-step cases initialize from signed seed -1,799,592,315, while creation returned -1,167,756,645. Their historical creation fields remain recorded; the fixtures now capture the actual seed before the first callback without changing any previous command, timing, view or event data. The native constructor uses that recorded callback seed, projected to the same 32-bit pattern.

In the real reset trace, the post-reset tick-63 seed is 1,976,269,105, but the intervening simulation step changes it to 1,344,433,435 before initialization at tick 64. The fresh opening is 141. The trusted host records that first-active seed explicitly and supplies it through `reset_with_seed`; the detached provider reads no Game and introduces no simulation RNG owner. Existing `reset` retains its stored seed when a caller explicitly wants that same-seed behavior. Native compilation, observation/policy composition, host scheduling and actuator acceptance remain pending.

The user paused development before the proof successor was complete. Its denial and cap/locality/resumption Source receipts are preserved, but the shared decoder and cfg tests remain unfinished integration and have not compiled. A metadata allocator retention patch had already been applied to prevent alert ID reuse across reset; its two-hurt/reset identity witness and independent review remain pending. The caller must retain its latest delivered view while commands remain in the private motor queue; absent-view fallback to Game is outside the supported contract. See `docs/checkpoints/2026-10-13-human-commander-pause/README.md` for exact frozen heads, known limits and preserved probes. This WIP introduces no new wire or gameplay authority and establishes no native human readiness.

`human_commander_state` carries the complete human wrapper phases independently of Match. It initializes only on the first active tick, remembers only newly delivered views, reads indexed owner hurts every active tick, carries alert identities across pending looks and queued causes, resumes interrupted commands untouched by a new plan, and consumes at most one physical input per tick. Planning markers wait for a view delivered after the marker reaches the queue front; planning precedes motor and input-cap checks. Camera, selection, key and click inputs all consume motor timing and cap space. Shared alerts are released when no runtime phase references them; owned alert and answer receipts preserve subscriber identity.

The planner callback receives camera-filtered rows plus its detached strategic packet. It rebuilds the existing policy input from those rows and collects provisional commands, matching Source's collector that returns undefined. Commands preserve raw shape through filtering and input construction. A physical input returns a delayed raw attempt rather than a claim of canonical acceptance. Later admission denial does not undo the consumed physical input, motor draw, recent history, last-place update or alert answer. Host queue admission failure is a different boundary: Root's whole-pass transaction discards staged human/policy/RNG state and issuer reservations, preserves the separately committed observation receipt, reports the completed simulation with an AI error, and fault-stops without retrying the tick. No second queue or authoritative Game read is introduced.

The unchanged Source runtime recorder exercises Easy, Normal and Hard openings through their first accepted Move and next physical simulation step. Normal's opening at tick 87 waits for view 88; its selection occurs at 88, click at 95 and movement at 96. The actual combat recorder issues a real enemy grenade command and lets Source step generate the hurt events. On-screen damage is noticed and answered at 36; off-screen damage moves the camera at 63, waits for view 64, then clicks at 92. Together the five cases contain 441 pre-act event batches, 12 delivered views and 12 physical inputs. Native component tests consume those actual views, indexed hurts and controls, with recorded Source policy output supplied only at the callback boundary. They do not simulate a native policy or actuator, and remain unrun. The full observation producer and native policy integration are separate; current typed ground Attack/Retreat admission is unsupported. Real resumption, cap and denial seams still need focused Source witnesses. Whole ordinary AI, server activation, wire delivery and Unreal acceptance remain unfinished.

The human observation seam separates producer-owned strategic memory from runtime-owned camera attention. `HumanView<P>` carries a detached strategic packet and ordered unit, visibility and sighting rows; the callback reconstructs its policy input from the camera-filtered rows rather than reading Game. `OwnerHurtBatch` preserves raw event ordinals and a generation changed on batch replacement, so filtering neither invents nor duplicates alerts. Current owner-out and winner controls are explicit public caller facts. Initial spawn and recorded seed are supplied for dormant state creation; the first active tick starts its opening timer. Malformed coordinates, including z, are outside the supported finite Source envelope and must fail before state or observation receipt publication. These internal facts introduce no wire, queue or persistent gameplay authority. Full state, producer and policy integration remain in progress.

The next detached human provider retains original command shape through duplicate filtering, formation merging and camera/select/key/click construction. Shape matters: a plain `t/orders` Move may be filtered or combined, while a Move with extra fields passes through. Ordered and repeated IDs remain intact; selection comparison uses a sorted key, screen edges are inclusive, and missing IDs never invent positions. The canonical host remains responsible for parsing and admitting the later physical command. These helpers own no queue, Match, vision, wallet or policy. Thirty-one unchanged Source cases compare 79 emitted input rows. Malformed inputs fail before cursor publication. Native helper tests, alert scheduling, private timing integration, delayed admission feedback and the complete human brain remain pending.

Independent source review caught a long-run difference in the conventional wrapping Mulberry32 implementation: the original keeps its Number accumulator and rounds it above 2^53. The native provider now retains the accumulator's floating-point bits and converts only the bitwise mixing input to uint32. Three original fractions at draws 4,917,758 through 4,917,760 protect that boundary. This preserves Source behavior without introducing a match RNG purpose or changing the policy stream; its focused native test remains pending.

Ordinary rooms select `shared/ai-human.js`, which uses `ai.js` for decisions and independently schedules camera attention and inputs. The fixed scripted planner is not a replacement for that brain. `human_commander_timing` ports the complete private timing leaf: the source's slot-derived Mulberry32 seed, difficulty values, two-draw lognormal samples, reaction floor, load-adjusted scans, tick rounding and view demand. The recorded match seed is supplied explicitly; this private stream does not borrow the simulation or policy stream. Core math supplies the original log, cosine, exponential and square-root behavior.

The recorder appends four private exports in memory to unchanged Source bytes and also calls the original public opening, camera, demand and reset functions. Its 48 profiles compare 336 sequential sample values and rounded ticks, with 14 private draws per profile. Eight public openings confirm demand one tick before the opening deadline; two records confirm that difficulty changes preserve an existing deadline and reset reseeds it. Those public records corroborate Source behavior, rather than proving an implemented native camera/reset runtime. Native tests are pending an independent source review and CI lease. Camera, alert ownership, input queues and caps enforcement, screen-limited policy, command feedback, transport delivery and Match call sites remain unfinished. No AI parity row is marked complete.
## Detached human commander observation (2026-10-13)

Prepared observation publication replaces the recipient's owned routing arrays. The additive `RecipientRoutingCommit::commit_with_paths` rebinds the existing `Match.commands.paths` cache to that prepared storage after every fallible packet and receipt check, immediately before infallible publication. It retains graph versions, vehicle buffers and search history and does not search or clone the path cache. Rebinding matches the selected recipient's prior storage and leaves other identities unchanged. Consumers that actually searched a prepared fork continue to publish that searched fork. Two unchanged-source route-history scenes retain incremental edits, same-key observations and a second recipient's routes; the native regression also checks refused observation publication. Native execution remains pending.

The private observation memory's initial-array address, dimensions and seed are a live binding guard. They are not a durable match identity after deallocation and allocator reuse. Callers must reset this private owner at handover or a new match. This producer uses the original default AI delivery boundary (even tick or final winner); adaptive server-supplied delivery and all-wanted-seat admission remain separate caller work.

The new `match_human_observation.rs` seam supplies the human wrapper with a complete existing `commander_ai::Observation`, plus its actually delivered ordered unit/visibility facts, optional own move and attack-move destinations, and original-view sightings. `HumanObservationMemory` mirrors the private `ai.js` observation memory outside Match. It is distinct from HumanState's camera attention and the existing policy's SourceState. The producer also retains actual ai-view stationary positions and since values, with original visibility loss and tolerance. The quiet strategic Input has no firstStillAt field, so its use by a broader artillery policy remains pending. Sighting updates retain first discovery order; strict 60-second expiry and later rediscovery retain the source Map behavior. The policy callback must rebuild its filtered Input from this owned packet and camera-filtered rows, never from Game. The pure human_screen_observation helper validates that handoff and retains all other packet fields unchanged.

The existing strategic construction body is shared through a pure helper. Its old opening wrapper keeps its original refresh and field behavior. The human wrapper supplies initial public map facts and accepted preview rows: known mine characters remain `N`, while flags use the received ground/object surface, as ai-view does. It stages only canonical recipient routing/Fog and its private view-memory component, then validates the complete packet before publishing the existing observation receipt. No whole Game or Match clone, gameplay grid mutation, path scratch, delivery cursor or simulation draw is used.

The live stop controls read canonical current seat out and Match finish independently of delivered-view age. Hurt projection borrows the caller's complete ordered typed shot frame with explicit generation. Non-hurt rows keep their original ordinals; actual owner Hurt rows are selected before the private camera/area policy. Appending keeps generation; replacing the source frame changes it. Firing, support and environment adapters read their existing typed events without creating or draining another event store. Finite x is the original alert admission condition; an admitted nonfinite z is an explicit malformed-input error before state publication.

The isolated source ancestry normally combines checked commander `be1cb2c` with checked ordinary Default `262926e`, then the human timing/raw-input/common-fact prerequisite `e91ac3a`. Early Cover and Entrench dispatch remain disjoint existing branches. Original unchanged ai.observe/viewFor bodies and the unchanged human hurt guard expression record four ordinary Default/Conquest scenes with and without Tech, 48 delivered views and three reset/append hurt batches. Three focused native tests cover complete rows, private perturbation/detachment, reverse paid-job parity and atomic refusals; they are written but unrun. This source milestone does not establish a full strategic policy, a Match AI tick caller, event-frame assembly, construction target projection, World or other modes, off-map AIR, save/restore, transport, wire or ABI adoption. Independent review and a leased coherent native proof remain required.

## Construction in the ordered caller (2026-10-13, source assembly)

The separate caller connects the reviewed construction provider through its real frame API. Pure paired-state admission runs before commands and again after command publication. The temporary frame starts after the existing path scheduler and firing claims; each reached ordinary actor contributes immediately after autocast and before project/Dig work. Ordered site completion and repair run after the actual unit loop, before the late unit membership is captured and before separation. The caller reports Construction once after per-unit Firing. The frame owns no queue, clock, duplicated builder identity or mutable project authority.

The provider already validates and clears the canonical build target with its derived flag during accepted retreat and pre-drop cleanup. Only the blanket construction-retreat refusal is removed. Garrison, rail, boat and thrown-carrier exits retain their explicit admission boundaries. Construction emits no shots in the original command and crew bodies, so no event store is added; any ordinary environment outputs remain on the same chronological report. The 29-case original source fixture and actual 1,919-step consumer now use the chronological event API and await combined native execution. This assembly changes no frozen ordered-event or AIR proof and claims no complete room startup.

The separate construction/event assembly normally joins source checkpoints `625705e` and `c63e8ac`, preserving both histories. It retains the single command queue, path scheduler, ordered output API and paired builder cleanup. The actual construction phases and consumer event migration are attached; combined native proof remains pending, and the frozen ordered-event candidate remains independent.

## Research and production phase admission (2026-10-13, source candidate)

`Match::validate_production_phases(dt)` checks the canonical ProductionState, borrowed units and wallets before the caller advances its clock. Prospective research and individual building rows reuse the existing progression algorithms without publishing them. No Match, unit registry or wallet is cloned. Actual placement, observed rally routing and metadata publication remain at each later building turn. The guard accepts the existing World context and refuses queued AIR and enabled logistics until their actual publication consumers are connected. It does not replace full World terrain, actor or routing admission.

The pinned ordinary Conquest constructor has no mode object and its normal Buy path produces units immediately. A nonempty restored queue reaches `g.mode.suddenDeath` in the original building phase and cannot execute as ordinary Conquest. Inventing a mode object also changes the later income branch. The native ordinary guard therefore retains its explicit queue refusal. This is an architecture refusal of an unsupported restored scene, not completed training parity. Research advances after vision and before paid project recounting; real World or Classic queued training belongs at the completed building's live turn. The sole tick owner will wire those consumers separately.

The original recorder executes four complete traces, 2,429 steps, seven commands and two immediate births. Its 4,810 research observations retain the actual helper bodies and agree with unobserved full-state replay. The new human observation/state/planner work is not part of this published stack.

The earlier local acceptance remains exact compiled `e7f1b1f`: 25 affected debug tests, 227 locked workspace release tests, strict Clippy, formatting, release FFI and 50 independent copied-test executions. Those results do not establish the merged head. Fresh Linux, Windows and macOS checks on the published combined head are required before Root merges; Unreal acceptance and remaining privacy/product decisions stay separate.

## Why native (2026-10-11)

Measured in ww2-rts:

- **Server:** one battle's tick runs on one thread. A heavy Conquest battle (Six Fronts, six Massive AI, about 400 units) costs 16.3 ms mean and 34.0 ms p95 of the 50 ms tick in Node (`tools/bench.mjs`, ww2-rts#58). Bun is 1 to 14% faster. `WW2_SHARDS=N` (`server/shards.js`) spreads separate rooms over processes, but nothing splits one battle.
- **Client:** the browser's main thread draws, builds terrain and runs game logic. A three-human Huge World Conquest session fell to 0.7 FPS; after the ww2-rts#61 fixes it runs at about 30 FPS on an RTX 4060 with 12.6 ms of frame-loop JavaScript.
- **Crowds:** 2,160 soldiers near the camera run at about 35 FPS on Low and 21 to 23 on High in the ww2-rts#64 synthetic fixture; the GPU spends 22 to 41 ms a frame on that scene.

The goal is thousands of units per battle and a public release later. ww2-rts#64 concluded its own measurements don't justify a rewrite, so step 1 measures before anything is ported.

## Architecture (2026-10-11, pending step 1)

Paid construction uses `UnitControl.build_target` for the source's numeric site identity and `Unit.build` only as its synchronized activity projection. Sites reuse Game progress/health, World footprints/sections and ProductionState queues. Typed Build pays and stamps immediately even when queued, recording a singleton Assist with the snapped site center in the existing waiting queue. The path scheduler retains the source's live Build goal, including a present dead site until the actual worker turn clears it; exhausted retries drop only the site scalar and its projection. Accepted replacement and pre-drop retirement use the existing field owner cancellation with the paired construction cleanup. No paid site is refunded or deleted by replacing its workers.

One temporary ConstructionFrame captures site and worker membership at actual unit turns, preserving first-site and turn order. Its finish phase runs before the late captured unit list and does not reselect workers after movement or damage. Completion keeps the full crew/buildTime/tick expression even when progress clamps at one, so a site can need another repair turn before its workers clear their target. Finished repair uses the canonical section actuator and ordered provision debits. All touched logging layers, section reads, aggregate mutation/journal capacity and story charges validate before publication.

Accepted rare placement prepares a temporary physical World and maintained grid plus the recipient, Fog and path caches used by its crew. A private Arc shares the sole immutable InitialTerrain allocation; initial() stays unchanged. The checked cache fork rebinds recipient flags/heights and owned vehicle buffers while retaining graph versions, incremental history, serials and search scratch. The draft stamps levels and cells, creates the actual newborn and footprint graph, then routes crews in original order. Local routing acknowledges only draft pending records. The final physical guard binds dimensions, source allocation, revisions, ledger epoch/end and pending sets, then publishes into existing live terrain allocations. Wallet, story, ID and control writes follow all fallible preparation. Queued crews perform no route query and retain the original live routing caches. Immediate route publication clears only those crews' existing retry failure rows, as source findPath does; pending requests and failed/queued operations remain intact. Empty fires preserve the source's lazy Fog behavior; a same-key warm Fog stays warm with its original frame. There is no whole-Match or per-tick clone, new job authority, second queue, or RNG reset.

The unmodified source recorder covers 29 cases, 113 commands/scene actions, 1,919 full steps and 2,013 snapshots. Constructor seed pairing supplies the existing source draw from Match seed 701's Weather stream; runtime environment state advances naturally. A declared disabled-logistics reserve scene witnesses exact repair budgets, residual floats and the first-missing-reserve debit break. The native consumer is written against advance_tick, not copied phase logic, but requires the sole runner owner's construction wiring before proof. This checkpoint remains source-only until final source review and coherent native gates. The current caller admits only Default/Conquest with absent Game.mode and the eight static ground kinds. Depot nodes, coastal/cache providers, site cancellation/refund and Classic/World integration remain subsequent production work; no ABI, transport or player rule is changed.

Mass-project tick admission reads the existing canonical project and paid-work owners before the clock changes. Its temporary set verifies complete current actor membership without sorting the caller's turn sequence or keeping a captured actor store. Listed and archived active counters are checked under the exact source recount gate; stale queued Join numbers remain ordinary command denials. An unmodified full-step source recorder adds seven cases, 193 steps and 215 snapshots for actual payment, crew/sweep, death, completion and retry-drop timing. At `914efe7`, four debug admission guards, all 214 release workspace tests, strict Clippy, formatting and release FFI passed after both-profile package cleans. Independent copied debug and release binaries each passed the four guards. The source steps remain evidence for later sole-runner composition, with no native full-tick result or second tick implementation claimed.

Mass field projects use the single FieldWorksRuntime owner for unpaid ordered segments, unit membership and paid Dig records. Monotonic project IDs let BTreeMap retain source creation order; segment Vec order preserves middle-first allocation and strict first-distance ties within the next crew-sized window. A source-listed project can become archived while unit references still need its identity. Cancellation removes membership and paid work together without a refund, while cached active/crew counters keep the source recount timing. Typed Match admission and phase providers share the existing command queue and paid-cell executor. A failed Dig route drops only paid work; accepted replacement commands cancel membership too. The original recorder covers 216 plans, 40 prices and 25 traces (137 actions, 162 snapshots). Native comparison passes for 168 supported plans, 30 prices and 23 Match traces (131 actions); direct-helper and unsupported Wall cases stay explicit. Compiled `0f17e93` passed 13 focused debug checks, all 210 release workspace tests, strict Clippy, formatting and release FFI, with independent source and debug/release review. Full runner assembly, client previews and recipient works rows remain separate; engineering rows stay in progress.

Two programs talking over the network, as today. Only the rules core decides outcomes.

1. **Rules core (Rust, `rust/crates/core`).** Port of `shared/`. 20 ticks a second. The same seed gives the same match. Unit, weapon and map tables live in data files both sides read. No engine, networking or file-system code.
2. **Room host (`ww2-server`, Rust).** One thread per room; a battle's tick is split across cores and merged in a fixed order. AI planning and long path searches run on worker threads and apply at a fixed future tick, so the battle never waits and results stay deterministic. It runs standalone on Windows or Linux, or the game's Host button starts it as a separate program.
3. **Protocol.** Today's WebSocket protocol first, so the browser client works against the Rust server during the switch. Later:
   - your own units arrive as orders plus occasional corrections;
   - enemy units arrive as positions, only while seen;
   - binary and versioned;
   - UDP through relays for public release.
4. **Unreal 5.8 client.** Drawing, HUD, effects, sound and input only. It links the core through a C interface generated by cbindgen for the rules the browser client imports today: 56 names from `shared/sim.js` (55 still used) across 35 client files, 86 names from all of `shared/` across 40, such as `placementCheck`, `canBuild`, `priceOf`, `popCap`, `CELL`, `UNITS`, `CFG`. It also uses the core to move your own units between updates.

## Two repos (2026-10-11)

Axel chose a separate repo for the native rebuild. To keep the native game measured against the JavaScript game:

- ww2-rts stays the playable game, and gameplay changes keep landing there.
- `reference/ww2-rts` is a submodule pinned to a ww2-rts commit (`e4e579a` at creation). `PARITY.md` lists what that commit does and how the native game is measured against it.
- Moving the pin is its own commit, with the port of the changed rules and the affected `PARITY.md` rows updated.

## The JavaScript game is a prototype (2026-10-12)

Julio's direction: the JavaScript game was a prototype for trying the game's ideas out, and nothing in it is set in stone. The native game keeps every feature and its intent, and improves the architecture, engineering and rules wherever it can, instead of copying the JavaScript bit for bit.

- Inside the Rust core and server, improvements need only a record here. Changes players would notice also mark their `PARITY.md` row `differs` and need Axel's agreement, since issue #4 makes behavior differences a joint decision.
- Exact comparison with the JavaScript stays where it is cheap and finds port bugs: data tables, pure functions, single-component fixtures. Whole matches are compared by outcomes agreed before the run.
- Why: copying the prototype exactly would mean copying its accidents. At the pin, one unseeded random stream is shared by every room in a process, the AI's view of the battle refreshes on a beat set by measured server time, and some unit choices depend on the spatial grid's bucket order. None of these is a design choice, and each makes matches impossible to replay. `PARITY.md` lists the proposed replacements (decisions 1 to 8).

## Reference pin (2026-10-12)

`e4e579a` stays the working pin for step 1 (#2, #3) and for `PARITY.md`. Julio and Axel choose the porting pin together at the #1 decision (Julio's ack `julio-coordination-001` on #5). The only later ww2-rts commit on 2026-10-12, `d71532c` (#66), speeds up the browser client's drawing, adds an opt-in WebTransport motion lane and fixes save loading; its `shared/` edits give the same results by inspection.

## Thousands of units

A unit is a squad or a vehicle; an infantry squad draws 15 soldiers. Four pieces are needed, and a line-by-line port won't provide them:

1. **A tick that uses every core:** vision, movement, combat and snapshots split across cores, then merge in a fixed order.
2. **Group pathfinding:** a group heading to one place shares one flow field (a map of directions toward the goal), so cost follows destinations, not units.
3. **Orders instead of positions** for your own units, which keeps the host's upload small.
4. **Cheaper crowds:** animation baked into textures and less detail with distance. This needs measuring, because the ww2-rts#64 browser experiment that moved animation work to the GPU came out slower.

## Cheating

The server is the referee and each player receives only what their own units can see.

- **Blocked by design:**
  - map hacks: hidden units, terrain and enemy plans are never sent;
  - speed, teleport, resource and health edits: those values live only on the server;
  - illegal orders: refused whole;
  - reading enemy routes and targets: they go to their owner only.
- **Needs more:**
  - host cheating: host matches that matter on a dedicated or official server;
  - FPS aimbots: Easy Anti-Cheat at public release;
  - scripts and macros: rate limits only.

## Public release

One server program covers three ways to host:

- a friend hosting from the game, through Steam or Epic relays;
- community servers on Linux;
- official servers as containers on a game hosting service.

At release the game needs:

- Steam or Epic Online Services login;
- UDP through Steam Datagram Relay or EOS relays, which hide IP addresses;
- lobbies and matchmaking;
- Easy Anti-Cheat (free with EOS on Windows, Mac, Linux and Steam Deck);
- replays (seed plus orders) for cheat reports.

Decided now so release stays cheap:

- the connection sits behind one interface;
- a player is an id from a login provider, not a room token;
- every connection starts with a protocol version check;
- the server builds as a plain Linux program that fits in a container, so it can change hosts (Hathora, a game host, shut down in May 2026).

## Rejected (2026-10-11)

- **Lockstep:** every PC would hold the full state (map hacks), the FPS view would lag by the input delay, and Mac and Windows would need bit-identical math.
- **Unreal actors and replication for gameplay:** ties rules to the engine, tests need the editor, and a dedicated Unreal server needs a source-built engine.
- **Bevy client:** weakest where this game is heavy (UI, audio, effects), with breaking releases every four to five months.
- **C++ core:** links into Unreal with no bridge, but data races and memory bugs show up only at runtime. Rust rejects most at compile time, which matters for a multithreaded tick written mostly by agents.
- **Unity:** its Terms of Service (17.2(ff)) bar AI agents and MCP clients except through Unity's own authorized framework.

## Confidence and open risks

| Claim | Confidence | Settled by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Rules core outside the engine, server as referee, fog filtering | Very high | It's how the game already works |
| A native server fits more units into each tick | Measured: the straight port of the hot path is about 2 times faster than Node (17.2 ms against 35.7 ms per tick at 2,000 units, Measurements); more needs data layout and the parallel tick | #2 |
| The Rust core links cleanly into Unreal | High | #3 |
| Unreal is the right client (agent speed in Unreal, runtime terrain, crowd cost) | Good, not certain | #3 |
| Thousands of units | Unknown | #2, then the step 2 benchmark |

## Costs

- No playing from a link: Unreal dropped HTML5 export after 4.24.
- Windows builds are made on a Windows PC.
- Unreal 5.8 is the last planned Unreal 5 release; a move to Unreal 6 comes later.
- Two build tools (cargo and Unreal's).
- Determinism needs care: Rust's default hash maps iterate in random order, and neither Rust's platform math nor the `libm` crate gives V8's results (Measurements), so the core carries its own math module.

## Order of work

1. Two tests: #2 (sim) and #3 (Unreal). Decide here: go ahead, change the client, or stop.
2. Rules core and `ww2-server`, measured against the pinned JavaScript as `PARITY.md` describes; benchmark at 400, 1,000 and 2,000 units.
3. Unreal game: terrain, units, effects, HUD, sound, then the FPS view, with the browser client still working alongside.
4. Retire the JavaScript client and Node server in ww2-rts.

## World region capture and income ownership (2026-10-13)

The next bounded actuator keeps live World ownership in `WorldRuntime.regions`, commander wallets in the existing economy, capture counts in story and relocated homes in the existing player start rows. Temporary staged copies allow the whole operation to reject before publication when a consequence is not represented. The ordinary constructor and tick guards remain unchanged.

Source income precedes World capture. Ordered tick units determine upkeep, while an explicit current depot view determines node payment. `advance_income_with_depots` preserves that distinction without adding another Game lookup or economy table. The existing income wrapper derives current facts from its supplied unit view. The Match adapter refuses removed tick IDs until a producer can supply retained facts explicitly.

The capture pass distinguishes guard locks from numeric team locks, uses live grid order, and preserves neutralization, capture, story timing and the half-cell centers used by the source. Canonical `contested`, optional `locked` and `cap_tick` fields carry source state. Recipient region locks continue to use seen/remembered buildings instead of authoritative flags. Fort transfer, army/building elimination, common World finish and logistics-enabled execution remain explicit unsupported preflight paths. Twenty original-helper traces contain 39 transitions. The three region tests, three existing World startup tests and three economy tests pass in debug. All 167 locked release workspace tests, strict workspace Clippy, formatting and the release FFI build pass on source `ab7872e`; independent source/oracle review is clear. [Implementation boundaries](docs/runtime/world-regions.md) describe the producer inputs.
## Quiet Conquest opening policy (2026-10-13)

`commander_ai.rs` implements the ordinary Conquest opening point-selection component from `shared/ai.js`, using an owned, validated observation. It preserves the scout aim, income and distance score, headed-unit load, capped failure memory, first infantry holder, busy-unit checks, neutral capture moves, uncapped cut-point attack-moves and cover-cell or circular waypoint selection. Scores and waypoint math use core math. Stable unit IDs and objective order define ties. The source oracle calls the unchanged exported thinker with actual decoded observations, explicit component overlays and a recorded fraction tape; it disables economic branches through the observed zero budget rather than replacing source helpers. The fixture retains pending holder cover/garrison outputs separately and currently records one cover order, so the movement comparison does not imply a complete thinker input log.

`match_commander.rs` is an explicit authority-to-observation boundary. The driver refreshes canonical vision only at source ticks 1, 5, 9 and onward, skipping a pass already applied by another Match caller. Ordinary startup has no initial vision pass. Its fallback vision pass uses a fresh query index and preserves the maintained gameplay index. Delivery requires that canonical phase rather than taking an extra look. The producer reads the recipient's ordered remembered terrain, masks authored mines using their public ground/object layer, and projects source public position, HP, suppression and visible-target precision. Private path and relevant busy-plan facts belong only to the owner. The policy cannot borrow authoritative state. Its two-tick delivered frame remains owned and unchanged between deliveries. Refreshing recipient observation state is separate from publishing commands; queue rejection commits neither seat policy memory nor its purpose RNG.

The bounded driver uses the source scripted planner's easy/normal/hard intervals of 120/40/20 ticks and seat stagger of 13 ticks. It replaces the prototype's load-dependent snapshot cadence with a fixed two-tick beat, and replaces shared `Math.random` with `StreamId::Ai(slot)` from the recorded match seed. These follow the deterministic replacements in PARITY. Commands enter the canonical bounded queue at the next tick boundary with explicit owner/sequence keys, then use the same Match command validator as human moves. A caller must reserve an issuer's sequence space exclusively for this driver. The source's default seat brain is the human-like camera commander; this component does not claim that camera, look, reaction or input-queue behavior.

The driver explicitly defers after the quiet opening, on visible enemy contact or enemy objectives, and for injured, heavily suppressed or unsupported own profiles. It does not shop, spend support, retreat, fight, fortify, run staged assaults, project complete economy/logistics/aircraft/job state, preserve AI state in saves, plan World strategy or schedule a complete Match tick. Positive-budget shopping is a pending policy branch, not a player rule change. Full AI rows remain in progress until those branches and a seeded complete-match input trace are verified.

## World Match startup ownership (2026-10-13)

The layered loader and full generator precede the executable World startup lane. `Match::new_world` accepts an explicit validated map and the generator's retained `MatchRandom`; ordinary construction keeps its World refusal. World homes resolve their ownership from actual commander teams rather than generator hints. HQs, local bases and guards, forts and starting commander forces follow the original setup order through the shared staged unit factory. No second unit factory or authoritative unit/health table is introduced.

`WorldRuntime` owns only mutable region team/progress/capper, resource node state, startup rail state, remembered building rows and the local defender actor's faction/spawn/visibility. Immutable geography remains in `World`, currency in the commander economy and queues in production. Fixed internal owner `0xffff_ffff` represents the source's array property `players[-1]` without adding a lobby seat, wallet or result participant. It is admitted only by World publication and actor lookup. Guard-home and ability/retreat switches stay in canonical controls; position and hold state stay in the unit. Source order is retained for ghost teams, visible-unit deduplication, rail masks and unclipped signed yard cells.

The bounded startup initializes real World visibility but does not collect remembered terrain or register terrain recipients. Logistics-enabled startup refuses until the World regional supply graph, stores and recipient routing are connected; the ordinary ground network cannot replace them. Legacy objectives/scenarios, World commands, live mode phases, combat actor integration, trains and server hosting remain separate. Malformed rail references reject before a match is returned. This is a defensive internal input boundary, not a wire contract change.

The authoritative region accessor preserves raw membership indices and the source's floating-column fallback, including partial/nonmultiple dimensions and signed IDs. The economy projection combines mutable runtime teams with immutable exact kind labels. Rail and station fields in terrain rows are explicit snapshots supplied from the canonical rail owner; only rows created after setupRails are annotated, with complete validation before mutation. Other mutable-terrain writers still need that shared projection hook.

Five original `createGame` records cover bare/dressed Huge/Massive maps and one crafted visibility/rail/home-team input with 2,761 units. The tests compare source units, canonical controls, region/home ownership, wallets, production, terrain fingerprints and ordered logs, rail setup and both commander/local visibility. Generator-to-constructor replay checks retained Terrain-stream state. The first native run exposed missing rail/station log fields; the repaired merged source at `800023a` now passes the original records, regional queries, combat/firing metadata checks and invalid-input atomicity. ## Supply stores and convoy runtime (2026-10-13)

`supply_convoys.rs` ports the pinned `shared/supply-convoys.js` store, fleet, scheduler, service, delivery, command and recipient-projection functions as a standalone component. `ConvoyState` owns ordered store inventories, payer buckets and donation grants, truck jobs and cargo, scheduling queues, losses and events. Unit positions, owners, hit points, movement and wallets remain caller-owned. `LogisticsState` is still the single reserve authority; territorial service adds optional `supply_rate` and `cut_for` to that record, initially absent. Store locations are the source's explicit 40-tick synchronization cache.

The `World` adapter supplies current unit facts in authoritative insertion order, physical handoff, territorial supply rates, route planning/continuation and immediate typed spawn, retire and movement effects. Operations must be visible to later queries within the same call. Sources, caches, parked empty halftracks, aid hospitals and depot points retain the source lifecycle, stock order and source refill rules. Private stock excludes grants, and automatic fleets cannot re-carry allied donations. Local service runs before territorial service, including their overlapping refill behavior. The source's constant capacities, dispatch/load/unload intervals and route budget stay beside this module and are checked through original-helper traces rather than a second catalog.

Typed context validates dimensions, owners, finite coordinates/population, nonnegative finite time and a safe JavaScript tick range with room for the longest retry before changing state. Support spending resolves a canonical unit ID and rejects negative or nonfinite amounts before any spending. Unit facts, restored convoy records and low-level store stock remain trusted internal inputs, not an untrusted-save parser. Ordinary movement interception marks the convoy held/manual; the caller applies the ordinary unit order. The unused legacy World region-cache store form is excluded explicitly.

The recorder executes original functions with deterministic route, continuation, handoff and territorial-rate hooks. Twelve Conquest, Classic and World traces cover zero-time setup and 540 runtime steps, plus six payer-pool and five support-spending cases. Complete ordered internal state and two recipient projections are compared, including finite donations, population scaling, clustered member order, route failures/retries, fleet growth/retirement, manual/resume/death, team changes during unloading, on-course route reuse and stuck-route repair. This proves the component for those explicit hooks. It does not implement the supply-network flood, actual safe-routing/handoff algorithms, match orchestration, forced withdrawal, World region-rate projection or persistence/wire serialization. The Rust server is not linked to this component, and no Game fields, ABI or public rules hash change.
## World dressing and rail generation (2026-10-12)

`generate_world` extends the base geography stage with optional `dressWorld` behavior and the source rail overlay. Biome selection follows terrain statistics and seeded provinces. Town placement preserves objective reserves, roads, region membership and one-cell building lanes; authored church, factory and mine-head tags remain typed data. Full output transfers these tags, optional region/cell biomes and rail metadata through the agreed World map input; layers remain absent to use the source rows-only migration. Coordinate hashing and separate pick/town Mulberry32 streams keep dressing isolated from relief, channels, territories, homes and names. Heights remain unchanged. Rail station search and route costs use core math, float32 coarse costs, score-only heap ties and insertion order. Rails never consume random draws or mutate terrain.

The source rail router blocks water, fords and houses at sampled two-cell nodes, then rasterizes between them without checking every intermediate cell. The native port preserves this known behavior. It does not invent a rail repair or balance rule. Native full generator version 2 identifies the extension, separate from base version 1 and the prototype full generator version 3. Generated-map validation, World Match startup, recipient projection and live rail operations are separate integrations.

An independent source recorder captures 20 Huge/Massive maps across five seeds with scenery both enabled and disabled, including cell and region biomes, 741 landmark tags, 1,032 stations and all 1,238 ordered rail-path fingerprints (140,906 raster cells). When scenery is off, the source omits landmark tags; the owned native loader input uses an empty list with the same authoring meaning. It also executes the original static terrain initialization before Match setup and records flags, house kinds, health and wear; the typed generated input goes through `AuthoritativeMap::from_world` and `World::from_map` for that comparison. Tests compare the original records and check the source stress tool's 80 settings for seeded replay, geography isolation, wide corridors, neutral spacing, flat home reserves, flat stations and rail continuity. The five generator tests pass in debug and release, including all 20 original-source cases through the real loader and all 80 dressed settings generated twice. Strict workspace Clippy and formatting pass. Independent final source review is clear.

## Base World geography generation (2026-10-12)

`world_generation` generates the bare geography stage in the core, without file, network or engine access. Huge is 512 by 512 cells with 64 regions; Massive is 1024 by 512 with 128. Each region begins in a 64-cell province. The implementation follows `world-conquest.js`, `world-landforms.js`, `world-roads.js` and the territory growth in `world-territories.js` at the pin: substantial relief with two armor passes, one to three main rivers, zero to three fordable tributaries, a connected lake, two bridges and two fords per main river, guarded objective reserves, a proximity road network, wide road verges, woodland and city blocks, distant homes, unique names and weighted cell membership.

`generate` takes the match's recorded `MatchRandom`, consumes exactly one Terrain draw and retains its low 32 bits as the geography seed. Weather, combat and AI draws cannot affect the result. `generate_from_terrain_seed` replays that explicit geography seed. The source's main Mulberry32, independent hydrology Mulberry32 and independent name LCG operate only within generation. This preserves the landscape algorithms without adopting the prototype's global random stream. Invalid player/team requests draw nothing. Inputs are bounded to one through six seats and team IDs zero through five, consistent with native match inputs.

Core sine, cosine, arctangent, hypot and rounding decide every threshold and route cost. Road costs remain float32, while their heap scores and territory distances remain float64. Score-only heaps, stable proximity sorting and insertion-ordered backbone traversal preserve the source's ties. The source's foliage phase intentionally uses decimal `6.283`; substituting TAU would move woodland thresholds. All output is owned typed data in cell coordinates. `into_world_map_input` moves the terrain and regions into the shared `world_map` records, retaining waterways as authoritative metadata. It leaves layers and biomes absent to select rows-only initialization, and supplies no rails. Loader validation remains a separate dependency. The seed and hydrology diagnostics are authoritative data and do not define a public map response.

The independent recorder invokes the original generator with `scenery: false`, then records ten Huge/Massive maps for seeds 0, 1, 42, 20261003 and 4294967295. Fingerprints cover every terrain character, height and region-map cell; direct metadata comparisons cover regions, homes, teams, names, river path hashes, crossings, lake and passes. A second check uses the source stress tool's 80 settings across both sizes, one through six players and mixed teams, generating each twice. Independent cardinal walks require every region to remain reachable with every bridge destroyed and a three-cell-wide clearance corridor. Channel steps stay contiguous at zero height, tributaries join and widen their parents, homes have neutral expansion 50 through 70 cells away, and comparable flat territory reserves leave at least 20 candidate 3-by-3 building footprints after excluding the home HQ. These are geometric construction checks, not full placement or economic pacing tests. No performance claim is made.

This milestone stops before `dressWorld` and `railNetwork`. Native generator version 1 identifies that partial stage, distinct from the prototype's full generator version 3. Biomes, landmark buildings, scenery isolation, rails, World startup, full construction fairness, privacy projection and multiplayer acceptance remain queued work. Related parity rows stay in progress where those pieces are required. The stage does not alter a balance value, the reference pin, C ABI, rules hash or wire format.

The first slice covers trench, sandbags, wire, tank traps, Gun Pit, minefield, known-mine clearing and Field Hospital. Dropping the guard spends nothing. Refused replacements preserve paid work; accepted cancellation has no refund.

Work retains the source's lazy initial cell count, engineer half-time, fixed-time accumulation and at-most-one cell per call, including large time steps. Every cell is rechecked at completion. Trap occupancy follows the original test over all non-infantry units, including units not currently alive. Mine placement preserves its underlying layers; mine clearing restores them. Dug fronts use the core math and float32 assignment. A completed paid job returns the exact owner's built counter request, even when its last cell was skipped. Canonical activity derives from the dig record, not a second control boolean.

The original-source recorder captures 22 traces and 367 actions, including twenty-hertz progress, skipped cells, hidden plans, mine knowledge, hospital limits, cancellation, cached distant approaches and placement boundaries. Its path and sight callbacks are explicit hooks. Dispatcher and tick integration, actual Dig scheduler eligibility, garrison exit and story commits remain pending. ## Supply stores and convoy runtime (2026-10-13)

`supply_convoys.rs` ports the pinned `shared/supply-convoys.js` store, fleet, scheduler, service, delivery, command and recipient-projection functions as a standalone component. `ConvoyState` owns ordered store inventories, payer buckets and donation grants, truck jobs and cargo, scheduling queues, losses and events. Unit positions, owners, hit points, movement and wallets remain caller-owned. `LogisticsState` is still the single reserve authority; territorial service adds optional `supply_rate` and `cut_for` to that record, initially absent. Store locations are the source's explicit 40-tick synchronization cache.

The `World` adapter supplies current unit facts in authoritative insertion order, physical handoff, territorial supply rates, route planning/continuation and immediate typed spawn, retire and movement effects. Operations must be visible to later queries within the same call. Sources, caches, parked empty halftracks, aid hospitals and depot points retain the source lifecycle, stock order and source refill rules. Private stock excludes grants, and automatic fleets cannot re-carry allied donations. Local service runs before territorial service, including their overlapping refill behavior. The source's constant capacities, dispatch/load/unload intervals and route budget stay beside this module and are checked through original-helper traces rather than a second catalog.

Typed context validates dimensions, owners, finite coordinates/population, nonnegative finite time and a safe JavaScript tick range with room for the longest retry before changing state. Support spending resolves a canonical unit ID and rejects negative or nonfinite amounts before any spending. Unit facts, restored convoy records and low-level store stock remain trusted internal inputs, not an untrusted-save parser. Ordinary movement interception marks the convoy held/manual; the caller applies the ordinary unit order. The unused legacy World region-cache store form is excluded explicitly.

The recorder executes original functions with deterministic route, continuation, handoff and territorial-rate hooks. Twelve Conquest, Classic and World traces cover zero-time setup and 540 runtime steps, plus six payer-pool and five support-spending cases. Complete ordered internal state and two recipient projections are compared, including finite donations, population scaling, clustered member order, route failures/retries, fleet growth/retirement, manual/resume/death, team changes during unloading, on-course route reuse and stuck-route repair. This proves the component for those explicit hooks. It does not implement the supply-network flood, actual safe-routing/handoff algorithms, match orchestration, forced withdrawal, World region-rate projection or persistence/wire serialization. The Rust server is not linked to this component, and no Game fields, ABI or public rules hash change.
