## Ordinary Conquest supply integration

Ground supply borrows authoritative terrain, its immutable initial snapshot, objectives, live unit facts and player state. It reuses the existing zone-of-control helper and MOVE-only spawn placement. The source bucket queue and Uint16 distances remain explicit, including damage cost, source selection and the best open distance within three cells. `Match.supply_net` owns the derived network, while optional `Match.convoys` owns stores, grants and truck jobs. `Match.logistics` remains the single reserve owner.

The real convoy adapter borrows canonical unit controls, firing and combat metadata, recipient route knowledge, terrain and the spatial grid. Queued destinations come from validated per-unit movement commands, preserving every waiting destination. Move and Stop first apply the source convoy manual/hold interception, then continue through the ordinary command actuator; manual supply, Resume and physical provision spending have typed Match methods. Unsupported queue projections and unrepresented garrison-entry facts reject before convoy mutation. Rider identities come from CombatState. Private route planning uses remembered terrain and visible weapons only. Automatic routes retain the source danger-mask, ordered incremental changes (32 records) and 40-tick threat-cache rules; physical handoff uses authoritative infantry connectivity and building-edge clipping. Truck creation executes source scatter and vehicle/occupancy clearance, then publishes canonical unit/control/firing/combat rows and updates the grid immediately.

Initialization is an explicit phase after troop, base, scenario and metadata setup: initialize every eligible reserve, build the ground network, then call the actual convoy runtime at zero time. Early network recomputation and late convoy stepping are separate executable phases. Reserve time and actual driving-fuel charging also remain separate. The constructor now calls this initializer after metadata and recipient setup. The constructor matches 63 original createGame startup cases with 663 initialized reserve records. Manual and automatic runtime phases use the same canonical state. Forced withdrawal, garrison-entry handling, support recovery sites, other modes, wire serialization and server hosting are not implemented by this lane.

The source recorder reuses existing map/startup/objective inputs. Component tests compare 233 networks and 39,839 rate queries, 468 real routes and 324 physical handoffs. Startup records network byte hashes plus exact reserve, store and scheduling state. Three actual-hook runtime traces cover 873 phases and 72 checkpoints, including queued Move, Stop, Resume, physical provision spending and death. Arrival positions are explicit trace inputs, so this proves supply phases rather than the movement runner. Route fixtures deduplicate repeated terrain and include danger, ordinary, then danger searches within one cache epoch. Those searches retain distinct graph caches, matching the source view objects. Direct searches publish route endpoints and clear retry history; projected queued probes leave live metadata unchanged. Independent review also corrected path-clear identity increments, full initialization binding preflight, lazy recipient reads and ordered danger change history. Fourteen affected debug tests and all 137 release workspace tests pass. Strict workspace Clippy, formatting and the release FFI build are checked separately. No public C interface, wire fields, reference pin or rules hash changes.

## Executable Conquest command phase

`Match.commands` consumes recorded `OrderKey { tick, issuer, sequence }` input in the existing deterministic queue order. A command keyed to tick T applies while the simulation clock is T-1, before the runner increments it. Thus command outcome timestamps and recipient-view cache keys retain their source timing. Immediate internal callers, such as an operative, use `execute_command` at the current clock. Results retain the issuing key so the host can deliver a denial only to its issuer; this is not a network message schema.

The JSON adapter checks every supplied movement row before the source row cap and before changing any unit. It preserves the source distinction between a malformed whole command and valid rows selecting unowned or unavailable units. Move, attack-move, Stop and the three stance switches mutate canonical Unit and UnitControl fields. Waiting movement orders are limited to eight per unit, spread a shared facing destination before they wait, and resume through the same command actuator. Source forced-reserve eligibility and supply holds read the existing logistics records. Aircraft, other command kinds, automatic abilities, voluntary reactions and other incomplete unit jobs return explicit unsupported results or remain separate phases.

Destination settlement and leaving a house use authoritative physical terrain, as the prototype does. End spots retain source grouping, stable side sorting, capture-circle bounds, allied held destinations, cover seeking and facing. Route searches always use that owner's refreshed observed terrain, live filtered hazards and canonical Match path scratch. A blocked first waypoint can try the source's three open-cell alignments on remembered terrain; no search falls back to hidden authoritative terrain. Every failed alignment contributes to the scheduler's failure outcome even when a later alignment returns a route.

The early per-unit command phase completes a reached world goal, asks for a missing route and starts waiting orders before timers run down. The attack-move phase runs after timers: a shootable selected target stops its path, a cleared target resumes it, and proximity below 2.5 metres ends the order. The movement adapter passes the temporarily detached active Unit to the same scheduler for infantry recovery and searches immediately for vehicle or traffic replans. Empty recovery and immediate-replan results preserve the old route and its identity. `begin_path_tick` must have completed on the current tick before movement starts; a later search error aborts the tick and is not safe to retry after partial movement. Match movement now supplies the recipient-memory traffic adapter described below. Automatic reactions and the full match loop remain separate work in this checkpoint.

The pinned original command bodies currently provide 56 traces and 246 recorded steps across four terrain layouts and every non-air type. They cover ownership, malformed rows beyond the cap, queued facing, duplicate unit rows, queue overflow, group pace, stance switches, forced reserves, stale job resets, garrison exit, short reversing, recent-hit state, hidden physical edits, budget deferral and stale queued paths. Two additional original movement replan branches protect empty-route retention. Three focused native tests pass in debug and release, comparing every recorded state and the two replan branches. Strict core Clippy and workspace formatting pass. Counter exhaustion rejects before command or movement mutation rather than wrapping a path identity. Movement reserves two increments because its route callback can replace the route before physical contact clears it again. These are component correctness records, not a performance measurement or a complete-match claim.

## Canonical generic hurt

Generic direct damage uses `DamageWorld::hurt` with a typed damage profile and falloff. It shares the existing explosion damage formula and canonical health, suppression, attribution and carrier mutations. The Hurt event captures the target's contact position before `jumpOff` can move a rider. Explosion callers separately add their radial death impulse from the post-hurt target position, while building-section selection remains outside generic hurt. This extraction is source reviewed and awaits the carried-target native regression; it defines no recipient or C representation.

## First-person infantry runtime

Generic direct damage uses `DamageWorld::hurt` with a typed damage profile and falloff. It shares the existing explosion damage formula and canonical health, suppression, attribution and carrier mutations. The Hurt event captures the target's contact position before `jumpOff` can move a rider. Explosion callers separately add their radial death impulse from the post-hurt target position, while building-section selection remains outside generic hurt. This extraction is source reviewed and awaits the carried-target native regression; it defines no recipient or C representation.

[FPS effects](docs/runtime/fps-effects.md) prepares exact drawn-body geometry and sampled rays, then commits canonical damage and shot state from the recorded Combat stream. Randomness is staged until damage admission succeeds. The externally observable Hurt-before-small-arms event order matches the source while the native transaction prevents malformed state from partially spending ammo or counters. The new source fixture retains intermediate JavaScript mutations, but native evidence must distinguish returned events/final state from unexposed intermediate writes. Typed grenade launch remains with the firing owner; source implementation is not native or whole-match acceptance.

`Match.companions` owns companion identities, accepted input, press counters, respawn times, vertical movement and loadout fields absent from the shared entity rows. Positions, horizontal velocity, health and suppression remain in `Game::Unit`; aim, following and autonomous switches remain in `UnitControl`; cooldown and target state remain in `FiringState`. Opaque companion keys are interned in admission order as nonzero numeric identities, so source string equality has one internal equivalent without changing a C or network representation.

## Quiet holder Cover continuation (2026-10-13)

The first idle infantry holder of an allied Conquest point now emits the source's `cover` order through the existing typed command queue. The policy reads only detached seat facts, including own manpower and remembered terrain characters. It refuses a holder with nonzero manpower or a nearby remembered house instead of skipping paid fortification or garrison decisions. Entrenching, garrisoned and already covered holders do not emit Cover, as in the source. This remains an opening policy, not the full commander.

The immediate actuator shares the existing threat, held-destination, cover-rank and recipient path helpers. Prepared routes are staged in one temporary Game copy so later selected squads see earlier destinations, while all paths are checked before committing units. Path identity increments are preflighted, including duplicate selected IDs. Denials retain the source's wrong-kind, retreat and no-cover order for supported states. Active dig, mass entrenchment, building jobs and garrison cancellation are refused before mutation until their canonical owner callbacks are integrated. Queued Cover remains unsupported.

Source generation and exact regeneration cover ten Cover command cases plus seven holder guards. The existing 55 opening cases and 246 command steps remain in the fixtures. Native tests, Clippy and combined integration are pending; no complete AI or fortification parity is claimed.

Cover consumes the same observation index correction as recorded recruitment (`0b397d3`) , full wreck observation (`ada6c4`) and recipient point visibility (`7699d8c`): each observation uses a local index, leaving gameplay bucket history untouched. A maintained test moves low infantry and heavy IDs out of and back into their bucket, then executes Cover, full wreck observation and recipient point visibility. Ordinary order `[2, 4, 1, 3]` and heavy order `[4, 3]` must survive those reads and a later squad path-capacity denial. The first squad's route refresh must not change the second squad's threat tie order. Path identity is checked before each route refresh. Native execution of this regression is pending.

Five more original-command rows cover mixed retreat/no-cover and wrong-kind selections, riding, operative and forced-supply refusals. The command oracle now has 71 traces and 261 steps. Independent review found a later-squad path identity error could still advance recipient memory and pending acknowledgments after the first route. The shared staged observer guard is being integrated before this checkpoint can be considered ready.

## Conquest ability and recovery component (2026-10-13)

`Match.abilities` owns source ability cooldown, buff and AP state, plus automatic/walked flags for an aimed order. `UnitControl.nade` is the only aimed point; Game owns sprint and health. `Match.recovery` owns previous health, wound/heal ticks and reinforcement progress. Logistics retains physical provisions. Startup and shared recruitment publication initialize these rows; stale metadata refuses admission before payment.

Recovery uses a captured late-list frame: prepare HQ/depot/aid membership, perform cadence-ten healing and mine sweeping, allow supply/network refresh, then reinforce against the prepared membership. It excludes later births and preserves earlier depot access on a beat that changes cut-off status. Medic choices retain maintained grid order. Firing owns flight geometry, IDs and random draws and borrows canonical buff/AP; the ability actuator creates no second projectile registry. These APIs are internal and add no ABI or wire contract.

Original-source recorders reproduce 36 ability traces (157 actions, 193 snapshots) and 23 recovery traces (133 actions, 156 snapshots) on x64 Node 24.21.0 at clean reference `e4e579a838aabae3b1d9dadbc17a0a87147bc52d`. Native replay compares 33 supported Conquest ability cases (147 actions), two explicit Classic refusals and an unsupported-project boundary, plus every recovery trace. Repair checks canonical current/historical sections, support membership, restoration, float32 cell health and ordered logs. Reinforcement uses the actual stock owner's cache-first provision hooks and preflights spending on the small story vector. Immediate medic route errors after earlier committed healing require the runner to stop; this component does not promise whole-phase rollback.

Ability input uses the existing recorded command queue. `Command::Ability` carries selected IDs, an optional point, queue intent and an internal automatic marker; the raw parser sets automatic false. Missing/ineligible IDs retain their original positions in refusal order. Queued aimed admission occurs before cooldown/ammo checks. Read-only resource, identity, work and garrison-placement preflight precedes selected control writes. Nade path requests use the existing retry/budget context. Smoke allocation shares the match allocator and rejects collisions with live units, clouds or wrecks before any selected effect.

The paired Combat purpose stream uses seed 701; the environment seed is separately 123. Grenade/satchel launch draws no Combat randomness, and barrage draws two values per round. Ordinary direct launches borrow the canonical ability row after the unchanged salvo branch: buff halves damage scale and interval and multiplies suppression by 2.5; qualifying non-infantry/naval AP sets launch accuracy to one and retains exact flags/multiplier. AP is consumed after validated nonempty launch commitment. Empty ammo and rejected ID capacity preserve AP and the stream. Beam execution retains its explicit unsupported boundary.

`Match.field_works` is the sole dig/pit owner; Game.dig is a synchronous activity projection. Accepted manual aimed replacement removes the descriptor before clearing this flag, without refund. Unrepresented build-target or mass-entrench project replacement returns Unsupported before control writes; a nonfinite aim preserves its earlier Blocked refusal. Ordinary field placement/dispatch remains the engineering adapter's separate integration. The field component's Point route projection and complete job snapshots compare separately.

Exact compiled runtime/test head `1f9a16b9299ad1bfcb99d2f530536c1bc69b765d` passes 13 core debug tests (including four ability and two recovery checks), three firing and two recruitment debug tests, all 177 locked release workspace tests, strict workspace/all-target Clippy, formatting and release FFI. Independent source review and copied debug/release ability/recovery executables pass on that same head. Later evidence/dependency documentation commits preserve the Rust/harness trees. Issue #138 tracks this bounded component; typed-flight #133, logistics #130 and FieldWorks #118 are dependencies. Full tick assembly, full engineering, Classic/World, generic mode enablement, transport, ABI and Unreal behavior remain separate, so affected parity rows stay in progress. ## On-map aviation actuator (verified component)

`Match.aviation` owns each aircraft's mission, station fuel, onboard ammunition, rearm timer, orbit angle and downed guard, plus sparse continuous-AA cadence and ammunition credit. Game owns physical pose, HP and PlaneState; UnitControl owns hold-fire and aim, FiringState owns cooldown/retarget/target, CombatState owns XP and last-hit attribution, and Logistics owns carried ground AA rounds. Query-only aircraft without sortie metadata return an explicit unsupported result before writes. Actual aircraft publication uses the shared staged factory and callback to initialize these canonical owners once. Purchase payment and production admission remain with production.

The bounded mission actuator admits held patrol and escort, and held fighter attacks against alive visible enemy aircraft. It tracks current target coordinates, retains the last known mission point when a target is lost, flies to station, orbits, bails home on source fuel/ammunition/health conditions, and restores HP/fuel/ammunition through rearming. Finished own airfields provide the nearest base; otherwise the source off-map HQ base is used. Aircraft automatic firing, ground-target guns and area salvos refuse before mutation because their weapon actuators are not available. Base, rearm and home stepping can proceed without invoking those missing phases. The existing canonical recorded command queue remains the sole eventual caller; no parallel input queue is added.

Continuous AA processes captured shooters in source order against current airborne grid candidates. It applies setup/building/hold-fire/visibility and forced/stranded guards, source shortage scaling, physical ammunition credit, XP arithmetic, attribution, cadence events and one plane-down tally. Staged target HP also governs later shooter eligibility, preventing a fighter killed earlier in the phase from firing. Equal-distance targets use the prototype's `<=` comparison over maintained grid registration order, including moved buckets, explicit re-registration and rebuilds. Airfield distance selection retains the first equal candidate. No alternative player-visible tie rule is introduced.

The unchanged original-source fixture records 18 aircraft constructors and 42 component traces with 3,410 checkpoints. Every helper call is recorded, including effect-free calls and completed rearming; no nominal-tick event sampling is used. External fixture pose/visibility/index inputs isolate movement and AA and do not claim full ground movement, vision production or lifecycle execution. The native consumer reads canonical runtime, control, Combat, Firing and Logistics fields and compares their floating-point bits, mission state, story and effects. Separate guards protect unavailable weapon phases and malformed late metadata from partial mutation. Source regeneration and independent source review pass. Vostro Linux x86_64 verifies exact code head `d159ca0bde9877bc306da853eacff405891d5bc5`: nine focused debug tests, all 174 locked release workspace tests, strict workspace/all-target Clippy, formatting and release FFI build pass after fresh four-package cleans in both profiles. The raw log ends with ALL_GATES_PASS and has SHA256 `dc08edaed83db06e0a0b9847b24d5abf8e5d0b54e9cc184ccb9d8ec0f0ef7913`; the exact source archive hash is `b51138d79b5a9b13927ec6014bf9563bfc9cfa88e6e3365e3792ede8154a92ee`. Two rejected Rust test literals and two inherited Clippy test-copy idioms were repaired without runtime or fixture changes. Recorded aircraft commands, retirement and sole tick consumption remain separate native-pending continuations. The bounded actuator has no full aircraft, complete tick, server, performance, C-interface or privacy claim.

## Bounded aircraft command integration (source checkpoint)

The single existing Command enum and OrderKey queue now represent aircraft Attack, Escort and Retreat alongside Move, Stop and Stance. `match_aircraft_commands.rs` prepares immutable aircraft actions against canonical sortie metadata and the existing mission admission rules. Every eligible selected aircraft is preflighted before convoy interception, source supply-hold updates, follow reset or command cleanup. A missing runtime row, queued aircraft request or unimplemented automatic/ground weapon path rejects the entire mixed selection before writes. Held fighter air-target attacks use alive visible enemy AIR; ground Attack and Retreat actuators remain separate and explicit unsupported phases. Escort ignores selected ground units after their source follow reset. Existing Stance can enable aircraft hold-fire and change neutral position/auto-retreat flags; disabling aircraft hold-fire remains unsupported until its automatic weapon phase exists.

Move/amove clamp points and create Patrol missions without facing settlement, terrain routing or recipient-memory reads. They preserve the plane's path, attack flags and Firing target while applying the source interruption cleanup. Attack applies the same interruption cleanup and creates the exact target mission. Retreat applies interruption cleanup and changes an airborne plane to Home. Stop clears only waiting orders and patrols at current pose when airborne; Escort clears waiting orders and follows its friend. Source sendPlane refusal during rearm or exhausted fuel/ordnance still preserves the old mission after command cleanup. Duplicate requests keep source row order. Ground Move settlement and pace use only ground requests, with the raw source cap applied before filtering aircraft.

Empty aircraft waiting queues are a no-op before aircraft stepping. Nonempty aircraft queues stay explicitly unsupported; no ground path callback is used to retry them. The unchanged original command function records 71 traces and 147 checkpoints covering all three aircraft kinds and five states, cleanup distinctions, clamped/duplicate Move, target denials, foreign/operative/riding selection, stale fields, malformed tails beyond the cap and the source command sequence. Native consumers compare actual canonical fields, preserve air path identities, assert no ground path requests, reject unsupported mixed selections atomically and replay permuted arrivals through the existing queue. Both source generators pass, independent review is pending and native command validation is pending. Full aircraft weapons, retirement and tick/server completion remain separate.

## Crater subtraction width (2026-10-13)

The original `dent` helper (`shared/sim.js:2742` at `e4e579a`) subtracts stored Int8 heights as JavaScript Numbers. Native crater digging now widens to i16 for the target and neighbor difference before applying the same minimum-level and slope gates. A stored -128 produces target -129 and returns without changing height. A neighbor at 127 above target -1 prevents digging. A neighbor at -128 beside target 3 permits digging, as the source does. These differences fit i16; the admitted target still fits i8 before storage. This fixes the actual overflow path without imposing a new terrain-value rule.

The existing original-source recorder primes correctly sized Int8 layers and runs actual blast damage through `dent`, touch and cell mutation. Four new traces cover minimum height, both extreme neighbor differences and valid level 4 lowering to 3. All 44 previous traces remain byte-equivalent as parsed cases. Generation and check pass with pinned x64 Node 24.21.0, now totaling 48 traces and 261 actions. The native environment replay consumes the same cases. Exact coherent code `76c817dd3d3ee70a1ada408e8c9543b784a11577` is verified on Mac Apple Silicon after four-package cleans in both profiles: mandatory environment/terrain tests pass all five cases in debug and release, all 184 locked release workspace tests pass, and strict workspace/all-target Clippy, formatting and locked release FFI build pass. Independent copied binaries pass all five focused tests in each profile. Debug exercises the former checked Int8 subtraction overflow; release exercises the former wrapping and wrong-slope outcomes. No source repair was needed. The proof directory `/private/tmp/ww2-aircraft-crater-76c817d-proof` records the exact source, commands, exits, logs, reviewer binaries and ALL_GATES_PASS. The merged runtime and fixture remain byte-identical to the reviewed crater provider, while the checked aircraft metadata, terrain guard and hooks remain intact. This component evidence adds no complete aircraft, production tick, transport or engine claim.

Missing/ineligible IDs retain their original positions in refusal order. Queued aimed admission occurs before cooldown/ammo checks. Read-only resource, identity, work and garrison-placement preflight precedes selected control writes. Nade path requests use the existing retry/budget context. Smoke allocation shares the match allocator and rejects collisions with live units, clouds or wrecks before any selected effect.

The paired Combat purpose stream uses seed 701; the environment seed is separately 123. Grenade/satchel launch draws no Combat randomness, and barrage draws two values per round. Ordinary direct launches borrow the canonical ability row after the unchanged salvo branch: buff halves damage scale and interval and multiplies suppression by 2.5; qualifying non-infantry/naval AP sets launch accuracy to one and retains exact flags/multiplier. AP is consumed after validated nonempty launch commitment. Empty ammo and rejected ID capacity preserve AP and the stream. Beam execution retains its explicit unsupported boundary.

`Match.field_works` is the sole dig/pit owner; Game.dig is a synchronous activity projection. Accepted manual aimed replacement removes the descriptor before clearing this flag, without refund. Unrepresented build-target or mass-entrench project replacement returns Unsupported before control writes; a nonfinite aim preserves its earlier Blocked refusal. Ordinary field placement/dispatch remains the engineering adapter's separate integration. The field component's Point route projection and complete job snapshots compare separately.

## Internal recipient snapshot projection (2026-10-13)

`recipient_snapshot.rs` reads canonical Match state through an immutable send-scoped cache. Every recipient gets its own row copies, and the cache's borrow prevents state from changing during one send pass. The first represented slice is ordinary Conquest unit rows plus economy, technology, rally, weather and finish fields. It has no `t: s` envelope and cannot be treated as a complete snapshot.

Ability cooldown supplies row column 11; the firing timer is a separate weapon cooldown. Aim and stances come from command state, ability flags from the ability owner, heal/reinforcement flags from recovery, and experience/cargo from combat. Missing owner state is an error. Air, engineering, gate and operative states are explicitly unsupported rather than projected from fabricated defaults.

Recipient rows follow the reference's cached `playerRow` path: hide unseen target IDs, clear non-owner cooldown, and preserve its existing non-owner flag mask. The uncached prototype path disagrees on several bits, so this does not adopt any pending privacy correction. The original Node recorder invokes `snapshotCache` and `snapshotFor` directly for 44 states and 132 recipient results. It covers owner, ally and enemy visibility, rounding, terrain and heavy-unit cover, vehicle tails, cooldowns and stance/ability/cargo flags. This fixture validates only represented fields. Terrain/fog delivery, plans/orders, points/effects, the complete snapshot and transport remain separate integration work; the server delta ledger is owned outside this module.

## Canonical movement snapshot results (2026-10-13)

## Conquest ground-unit reactions

`match_reactions.rs` supplies three points in the real Match unit loop: decrement the canonical UnitControl reaction timer with the other ground timers, apply automatic retreat before autocast, and react after ordered work but before movement. Game owns routes, position, health, suppression, drift and reversing; UnitControl owns stance, rally intent and orders; FiringState owns the target and volley claims; CombatState owns incoming shooter and hit time. No second reaction state or random stream is created.

Cover and spacing selection reuse the command destination helpers over authoritative physical terrain, public capture circles and allied held destinations. Route searches reuse the owner recipient routing actuator and never fall back to hidden terrain. Source row-major cell scoring and strict distance thresholds are retained. Idle spacing only shifts the newer own squad and never leaves covered ground for open ground. Short vehicle withdrawal preserves front-facing reverse movement. The full tick owner must exclude convoys and unsupported air, rail, operative and carrier phases before invoking these ordinary ground methods.

Conquest retreat chooses the nearest living, finished owned HQ, falling back to the player's spawn. The reset uses the existing canonical command/work fields and keeps hold-fire and hold-position stances. Automatic retreat retains the strict 35-percent health and 15-metre distance thresholds, including while hold-position is enabled. Garrison exit and carrier/air retreat require their own actuators and return an explicit error before the ground retreat reset. The helper does not report those modes as implemented.

Rally interception uses the firing owner's claim-aware target setter. Retreat follows the source's direct target reset, so an existing volley claim lasts until the next tick's rebuild. Path identity capacity is checked before reaction mutation instead of wrapping. The source recorder adds exports without changing the pinned function bodies and records 51 cases with hostile fire, crewed weapons, health thresholds, stances, pending work, rally walks, capture circles, spacing and retreat. Three focused native tests pass in debug and release, comparing all 51 source cases, target claims and float bits, plus unsupported exit, malformed-neighbor and counter-exhaustion rejection. The independent source review found and resolved timer mutation before fallible shared-state validation. Workspace formatting passes. At checkpoint ab0394d, strict core/all-targets Clippy stopped on inherited unused firing helpers terrain_contact_material and flight_contact. The stacked dependency now retains its checked contact driver; the final merged reaction checks remain pending. No suppression was added. Platform CI and full tick integration remain pending.
## Prepared unit publication (2026-10-13)

Owner-bound remembered routing is required for rallies; unreachable searches still admit an empty path as in the source. Air placement and pending supports without exact kind metadata fail explicitly before payment. [Recruitment design and evidence](docs/runtime/recruitment.md) records the transaction, control ownership, original-source fixture and remaining boundaries. No public interface or server match admission changed.

## Off-map support runtime (2026-10-13, bounded checkpoint)

`match_support.rs` owns ordered ground-support strikes and fighter-cover zones on Match. Player support cooldowns remain in PlayerStart, wallets in the existing economy state, smoke in canonical Game and identities in `next_unit_id`. Admission clamps target coordinates as the source does, rejects insufficient manpower or cooldown, and records accepted spending and calls once. Plane kinds return an explicit unsupported result before payment. This slice creates no second command queue or wire contract.

Artillery applies the existing support-class blast adapter before source dig=0 dent/crater behavior. Smoke consumes one OffMap draw per cloud and allocates canonical identities. Covers activate before cover timers decay. Support cooldowns decay without clamping; an allied uncut radio applies `CFG.radioCd`. Purpose-specific OffMap randomness replaces the prototype global stream, while each support effect keeps the source draw order.

The original-source loader now records 60 traces and 1,400 checkpoints for three ground supports, two faction doctrines, radio/cut effects, insufficient funds, existing cooldowns, clamped targets, invalid direction fallback and captured late-unit subsets. Three focused support tests pass in debug and release, with three debug combat and three firing regressions. All 158 locked release workspace tests, strict workspace/all-target Clippy, formatting and the release FFI build pass on the Mac. This is bounded ground-support correctness evidence, with no performance or full-match claim.

## Prepared unit publication (2026-10-13)

`UnitControl::new_for_mode` uses the existing compiled `ab_cost` rule for automatic ability defaults. The ordinary `new` method remains a Default-mode wrapper; the staged factory selects the actual Game mode. World startup and training share that initializer instead of maintaining another AB_MUN lookup. Two original-source spawn scenarios add six paid/free/absent-ability checks without changing the 55 earlier scenarios. This initializer is verified by 775 original-source spawn rows, including all six new mode cases, within the final startup workspace checks.

`prepare_research` keeps exclusive access to the canonical production state and payer wallet while leaving both unchanged. The Match can prepare the source MP-spent story update through a disjoint borrow, then commit wallet, lab and story without another fallible operation. The original component admission convenience consumes this same guard. Research costs remain catalog-backed, and completion neither spends again nor increments the construction-only built counter.

The original component traces include synthetic paid queues and explicit construction contexts. They do not establish legal Conquest queued production: the source Conquest constructor leaves its mode object absent and its buy command spawns immediately. The Match integration will refuse a nonempty Conquest training queue and use represented construction-mode startup for actual queued training. Split-operation tests reuse the original helper records and add dropped-guard atomicity. Native acceptance and Match actuation proof are pending.

## Shared finish and explicit Assault ending (2026-10-13)
## Shared finish and explicit Assault ending (2026-10-13)

`Match.finish` now contains one `MatchFinish` with a typed `EndReason`. Source reason strings include VP, HQ, bunkers, structures, timer, draw, World territory, surviving nations and tutorial completion. Defining those reasons does not enable those modes. A single `Match.timeline` contains `MatchSample::Conquest { t, vp }` or `Territories { t, points, structures }`; source-facing projections retain their original fields without a transport tag. Arrays use team IDs and preserve zero-filled gaps. Conquest scores are summed in player order and floored. Assault counts every owned objective and each live command bunker; flak, ordinary HQs, dead bunkers and neutral ownership do not add to team structure counts. The source rounds sample seconds and end coordinates through the deterministic math module.

The explicit `apply_assault_victory(dt)` implements the late Assault block after income and convoys. An undecided clock subtracts the finite elapsed value, including zero or negative values as the source arithmetic does. The last living bunker falling wins for the attackers before timer expiry can win for defenders. An attacker team of -1 produces a draw regardless of the requested structures reason. The final sample precedes canonical winner publication, reveal is enabled, and later calls freeze both the result and clock. No full Assault runner, elimination phase, scenario execution or server admission is enabled. The early sample remains a caller-owned phase; duplicate calls are not deduplicated.

Transport and ending-hold policy remain separate. Original-source evidence currently covers 11 Assault traces and 22 phases, alongside unchanged Conquest fixtures. Native verification is pending.

## Internal World recipient memory (2026-10-13)

WorldRuntime owns one lazy WorldMemory. Teammates share accepted terrain rows and the existing RouteKnowledge World projection; commander delivery cursors and per-owner live route caches remain separate. The fixed local defender can query internal routing without receiving a commander seat or snapshot cursor. The constructor starts this memory empty and resets the one source journal to absolute position zero after static structures.

Journal prefix trimming uses checked absolute positions. Legacy ordinary take_journal still drains and resets its epoch; active World memory uses journal_end and trim_journal_before. Invalid cursor ranges and exhausted end arithmetic reject before removing entries. Accepted rows retain first insertion order, and their revision changes only when source row content changes. Cover basis is captured at that accepted refresh and does not independently advance source row identity. Snapshot reads resolve the latest row at each change-log entry; route reads have their own cursor and fall back to full remembered order after trimming.

Discovery follows the existing current vision-pass fog mask, while wreck visibility queries current unit positions, grid candidates and line of sight. Reveal discovers terrain and wrecks but preserves hidden-mine filtering and the source's stricter current-visibility wreck removal. Region knowledge keeps canonical team/progress/capper/contested facts separate from immutable geography. Territory runs deliberately group raw region-map labels and look them up by region ID, preserving the source mismatch for signed or nonconsecutive IDs. This stage exposes internal refresh/query producers; World tick, command dispatch, full HUD/recovery, wire delivery and AI observations remain separate. Original-source and native component verification are recorded below.

World recipient entry points and the original-source 120-action evidence are recorded in [the runtime note](docs/runtime/world-memory.md). The explicit World visibility phase updates the existing canonical building ghost rows and local actor visibility; route reads do not advance vision. Rail and station row fields borrow WorldRails, which remains the sole metadata owner. A cached ascending visible-cell list follows the existing Fog key, matching the source visibleCells order without implementing a second fog algorithm. On runtime head 37094bd, the nine core debug tests, both World memory debug tests and all 164 locked release workspace tests pass. Strict workspace Clippy, formatting and the release FFI build pass; the original recorder reproduces four traces and 120 actions. These checks retain the explicit World integration boundaries.

## Eligible canonical World finish (2026-10-13)

The World region actuator prepares its finish only after the provider's ordered capture, fort-transfer preflight, home relocation and no-territory army preflight. It still refuses required fort/gate transfer, owned-unit deletion and building wrecking; those effects cannot disappear behind a victory calculation. Players with no territory and no owned units can be marked out completely. Players who already have an out flag and still hold territory retain their units, as the source does. No new elimination or fort-state authority is introduced.

`prepare_world_finish` reads the staged wallets and regions, preserving player insertion order for alive teams. Everyone out draws. Otherwise an alive team wins with all regions, or among rival teams with `ceil(total * WORLD_TUNING.winShare)` regions; only then can the last nation win. Huge uses 64 regions and a rival goal of 39, Massive uses 128 and 77. A single team needs every region. The decisive location is the first region in vector order whose capture tick equals the current clock, even when that region belongs to a different team; draw and missing-capture cases use the map center. IDs never substitute for region order.

Preparation owns its final sample/result without borrowing canonical story across publication. The actuator commits regions, wallets, homes and capture story before publishing the prepared finish and reveal without another fallible step. It retains the undecided-only boundary of the current provider. Later World tick continuation, concrete elimination/seizure and result transport remain separate work. The shared early sampler and internal result accept represented World through the same `Match.timeline`, `Match.story` and `Match.finish` used by Conquest and Assault.

World's source timeline counts physical `g.points`, which are empty on generated World maps; it does not report regions in that array. Its structures array counts every live structure, including unfinished buildings and units of out commanders, but ignores the local defender. No replacement region chart or new wire field is invented. The new unchanged-source oracle starts from actual World constructors before applying controlled scene facts, comparing 15 traces and 16 phases across both sizes. Existing World region evidence now accepts its three fully represented finish branches while retaining other refusals. Focused atomic checks cover malformed identity/story/timestamps/timeline and first-result preservation. Native verification remains pending.

The shared sample guard preserves the actor policy enforced by unit publication: the hidden local-defender sentinel is valid only in World. A controlled source helper can count an arbitrary negative-owner bunker outside that mode, but no ordinary native Assault actor represents it. The oracle retains that observation with an explicit unrepresented marker, and the native adapter rejects it before sample or clock mutation. This corrects an input-validation gap found in independent review; it does not change a valid lobby player's rules.

The Assault finish follow-up preserves the actor policy already enforced by ordinary unit publication. The hidden local-defender sentinel belongs only to World. ## Prepared World finish after removal (2026-10-13)

The World elimination consumer stages actual removal, building wrecks, environmental side effects, the maintained grid and retained routes before canonical publication. Ordered removed IDs complete the current unit set. The finish guard checks nonzero canonical map key/unit-ID binding, unique and complete coverage, unchanged survivor owners/kinds, finite HP and removal restricted to out commanders without territory. It then uses the same source sampler and finish preparation as the existing region phase. No duplicate units, story or timeline persist on Match.

Preparation returns owned finish/sample facts and mutates nothing. The region owner must commit the exact staged World/environment/grid effects and wallets/regions/homes/story before publishing that pair. This API does not simulate wrecks, transfer forts, remove units or authorize incomplete side effects. The original World recorder additionally records actual Map deletion order and post-effect unit values. ## Prepared routing sight (2026-10-13)

The reference publishes a produced unit before its first route query. Native recruitment prepares admission and placement before it publishes, so routing needs the admitted draft as an optional borrowed sight source. StagedObserver checks its future identity, owner, kind and finite pose. It contributes to current point sight and only to a team Fog build whose existing vision key is absent or stale. It never invalidates an already cached mask. Ordinary route keys and World per-owner live keys remain unchanged.

PreparedRecipientRouting borrows the canonical recipient and Fog owners and makes temporary component copies. PreparedWorldRouting does the same for the one WorldMemory owner. Their route queries read only these prepared recipient views; Game, authoritative terrain and the admitted unit are not cloned or temporarily inserted. Deferred receipts capture source sight facts and delivery keys. Final preparation checks the receipt and locks only the pending/journal ledgers, returning Game for disjoint unit publication. The routing commit is infallible after every admission, route, control, publication and story preflight succeeds. Dropping either preparation leaves canonical rows, buffer addresses, Fog, pending acknowledgements and journal prefixes unchanged.

Component copies keep the first implementation simple enough to audit and allow command batches to share the same observer-free transaction. They temporarily copy route buffers and, for World, other remembered team components inside the sole memory owner. No scaling or performance improvement is claimed. Search scratch that saw a canceled preview must be discarded: navigation cache identities include terrain-buffer addresses and knowledge versions, so reusing a freed preview address without invalidation could retain a provisional graph. Consumers may use detached per-transaction scratch and publish it only with a successful transaction. Original-source and native observer checks are pending.


Path search preparation forks the existing derived `Pathfinding` history rather than clearing its canonical caches. The copy retains externally borrowed terrain identities and all graph traversal order, route history and score state. Vehicle-clearance arrays are owned inside the cache, so cloned graphs explicitly rebind those array identities to the copied allocations. A failed transaction drops the fork. A successful transaction moves the fork together with the prepared recipient buffers, keeping their allocation identities valid. This copies cache storage for each preparation; no performance or scaling claim is measured yet. The canonical Game and World are neither cloned nor provisionally mutated.


A plain derived-cache clone still refers to the canonical recipient's old flag and height allocations. Searching copied preview arrays would rebuild the region graph and lose its source incremental insertion history. Each routing guard therefore exposes `fork_paths`: it clones existing derived history, then maps only the selected canonical recipient's arrays and matching vehicle-clearance arrays to the preview allocations. Cache versions remain unchanged until the ordinary incremental update sees the supplied change log. Unmatched recipients and authoritative terrain identities are never rebound. Successful publication moves both the prepared component and fork, preserving the allocations; abort drops both without resetting canonical search history.

The staged sight recorder calls the original ordinary buy command, which invokes `produceUnit`, and the original World `produceUnit` helper. It captures the owner view immediately after the original `knownTerrainFor` call inside `findPath`; original rules and search bodies remain active. Thirteen birth cases distinguish cold fog, prior empty-fire route reads, warmed fire/fog masks, same-clock and next-clock reads, infantry and vehicles, and World team memory. Two cases retain eight actual searches across ordered passability changes before birth. Shared raw setup and projection helpers permit the separate recruitment and World training consumers to reuse the evidence. The provider's typed publication tests cover its component transaction, not wallet/story/training actuator parity. Full recruit parity stays pending those integrated consumers.


Receipt preparation rechecks its captured World/Game observation facts and the affected pending set or journal cursor. Fire membership and mine ownership/discovery are included because their owners can change them without a terrain-version advance. Caller-owned context inputs such as ordinary building footprints and World rails, region/ghost tables, plus the draft's observation fields, must remain stable through commit. The receipt does not retain those external borrows or claim to validate subsequent changes to them. Path and publication metadata may be prepared separately without changing the admitted draft's sight facts.


World preparation copies the entire memory component. On success, the flags and heights for all existing teams move to the prepared allocations, so its search fork maps every copied team pair, including teams not read by that transaction. This preserves a warmed other-team vehicle cache after another team's birth commit. The expanded recorder covers fourteen births, twelve prebirth searches across accepted changes and one original postbirth cross-team reroute. Native tests exercise the other team's unchanged allocation on abort and its mapped history on success; execution is still pending.


## Prepared World removal retention (2026-10-13)

World elimination deletes owned units in source order, then wrecks each removed building. An earlier building collapse can change the final HP or position of a unit removed later. Owner and kind must match, with finite final observation fields; final HP and pose may differ from the pre-transaction unit. The guard holds only the memory component and copied cached-unit facts. Its infallible commit runs after deletion/wreck publication and replaces matching existing bound rows without changing membership, route buffers, terrain memory, visibility, outboxes or journal ownership. Drop preserves all recipient state. The removal actuator separately owns validation and atomicity of buildings, damage, nodes and rail/station row annotations. This helper does not establish elimination or finish parity by itself.

## Queued infantry Cover (2026-10-13)

`Command::Cover` now carries the incoming queue switch and an optional waiting-order display anchor. Incoming JSON supplies only IDs and the queue switch; its coordinates do not pick cover. The queue stores one normalized immediate Cover per selected squad, with the anchor taken from its last waiting order, then its last path waypoint, then its current position. It shares the existing eight-order limit and existing deferred dispatcher. Cover placement and recipient path reads happen only when the waiting order starts.

Admission stages each selected unit's order queue before publishing it, including repeated selected IDs. A later unrepresented anchor refuses the batch without partly appending earlier squads. Queuing does not advance path identities, alter live routes, refresh recipient memory or acknowledge pending terrain. Wrong-kind, ownership, retreat and full-queue results retain the source selection order for supported inactive, nongarrison squads.

Fourteen new traces execute the original queued command and waiting-order phase without modifying those bodies. They cover anchor priority, terrain added or removed before execution, eight-order capacity, mixed full selections, repeated IDs, retreat, permission filtering and a failed Cover draining before a following Move. The existing 71 traces remain unchanged. Native assertions cover queue admission isolation and malformed later-anchor refusal; native execution is pending. The immediate path transaction also uses the shared cache fork from `1400423` and lazy Fog correction from `178b603`.

Cover now requests its cache fork from `PreparedRecipientRouting::fork_paths` (`6d5f1e4`), which maps only the selected recipient's matching external arrays and copied owned vehicle arrays. This preserves warmed incremental history through the preview instead of treating copied buffers as unrelated terrain. Abort drops only the fork; success moves it with the preview receipt. Native proof is pending.

## Prepared routing sight (2026-10-13)

The reference publishes a produced unit before its first route query. Native recruitment prepares admission and placement before it publishes, so routing needs the admitted draft as an optional borrowed sight source. StagedObserver checks its future identity, owner, kind and finite pose. It contributes to current point sight and only to a team Fog build whose existing vision key is absent or stale. It never invalidates an already cached mask. Ordinary route keys and World per-owner live keys remain unchanged.

PreparedRecipientRouting borrows the canonical recipient and Fog owners and makes temporary component copies. PreparedWorldRouting does the same for the one WorldMemory owner. Their route queries read only these prepared recipient views; Game, authoritative terrain and the admitted unit are not cloned or temporarily inserted. Deferred receipts capture source sight facts and delivery keys. Final preparation checks the receipt and locks only the pending/journal ledgers, returning Game for disjoint unit publication. The routing commit is infallible after every admission, route, control, publication and story preflight succeeds. Dropping either preparation leaves canonical rows, buffer addresses, Fog, pending acknowledgements and journal prefixes unchanged.

Component copies keep the first implementation simple enough to audit and allow command batches to share the same observer-free transaction. They temporarily copy route buffers and, for World, other remembered team components inside the sole memory owner. No scaling or performance improvement is claimed. Search scratch that saw a canceled preview must be discarded: navigation cache identities include terrain-buffer addresses and knowledge versions, so reusing a freed preview address without invalidation could retain a provisional graph. Consumers use a fork of existing derived history and publish it only with a successful transaction. The component transaction now passes the original-source and native checks described below.


Path search preparation forks the existing derived `Pathfinding` history rather than clearing its canonical caches. The copy retains externally borrowed terrain identities and all graph traversal order, route history and score state. Vehicle-clearance arrays are owned inside the cache, so cloned graphs explicitly rebind those array identities to the copied allocations. A failed transaction drops the fork. A successful transaction moves the fork together with the prepared recipient buffers, keeping their allocation identities valid. This copies cache storage for each preparation; no performance or scaling claim is measured yet. The canonical Game and World are neither cloned nor provisionally mutated.


A plain derived-cache clone still refers to the canonical recipient's old flag and height allocations. Searching copied preview arrays would rebuild the region graph and lose its source incremental insertion history. Unmatched recipients and authoritative terrain identities are never rebound. Successful publication moves both the prepared component and fork, preserving the allocations; abort drops both without resetting canonical search history.

## On-map aviation actuator (verified component)

`Match.aviation` owns each aircraft's mission, station fuel, onboard ammunition, rearm timer, orbit angle and downed guard, plus sparse continuous-AA cadence and ammunition credit. Game owns physical pose, HP and PlaneState; UnitControl owns hold-fire and aim, FiringState owns cooldown/retarget/target, CombatState owns XP and last-hit attribution, and Logistics owns carried ground AA rounds. Query-only aircraft without sortie metadata return an explicit unsupported result before writes. Actual aircraft publication uses the shared staged factory and callback to initialize these canonical owners once. Purchase payment and production admission remain with production.

The bounded mission actuator admits held patrol and escort, and held fighter attacks against alive visible enemy aircraft. It tracks current target coordinates, retains the last known mission point when a target is lost, flies to station, orbits, bails home on source fuel/ammunition/health conditions, and restores HP/fuel/ammunition through rearming. Finished own airfields provide the nearest base; otherwise the source off-map HQ base is used. Aircraft automatic firing, ground-target guns and area salvos refuse before mutation because their weapon actuators are not available. Base, rearm and home stepping can proceed without invoking those missing phases. The existing canonical recorded command queue remains the sole eventual caller; no parallel input queue is added.

Continuous AA processes captured shooters in source order against current airborne grid candidates. It applies setup/building/hold-fire/visibility and forced/stranded guards, source shortage scaling, physical ammunition credit, XP arithmetic, attribution, cadence events and one plane-down tally. Staged target HP also governs later shooter eligibility, preventing a fighter killed earlier in the phase from firing. Equal-distance targets use the prototype's `<=` comparison over maintained grid registration order, including moved buckets, explicit re-registration and rebuilds. Airfield distance selection retains the first equal candidate. No alternative player-visible tie rule is introduced.

The unchanged original-source fixture records 18 aircraft constructors and 42 component traces with 3,410 checkpoints. Every helper call is recorded, including effect-free calls and completed rearming; no nominal-tick event sampling is used. External fixture pose/visibility/index inputs isolate movement and AA and do not claim full ground movement, vision production or lifecycle execution. The native consumer reads canonical runtime, control, Combat, Firing and Logistics fields and compares their floating-point bits, mission state, story and effects. Separate guards protect unavailable weapon phases and malformed late metadata from partial mutation. Source regeneration and independent source review pass. Vostro Linux x86_64 verifies exact code head `d159ca0bde9877bc306da853eacff405891d5bc5`: nine focused debug tests, all 174 locked release workspace tests, strict workspace/all-target Clippy, formatting and release FFI build pass after fresh four-package cleans in both profiles. The raw log ends with ALL_GATES_PASS and has SHA256 `dc08edaed83db06e0a0b9847b24d5abf8e5d0b54e9cc184ccb9d8ec0f0ef7913`; the exact source archive hash is `b51138d79b5a9b13927ec6014bf9563bfc9cfa88e6e3365e3792ede8154a92ee`. Two rejected Rust test literals and two inherited Clippy test-copy idioms were repaired without runtime or fixture changes. Recorded aircraft commands, retirement and sole tick consumption remain separate native-pending continuations. The bounded actuator has no full aircraft, complete tick, server, performance, C-interface or privacy claim.

## Crater subtraction width (2026-10-13)

The original `dent` helper (`shared/sim.js:2742` at `e4e579a`) subtracts stored Int8 heights as JavaScript Numbers. Native crater digging now widens to i16 for the target and neighbor difference before applying the same minimum-level and slope gates. A stored -128 produces target -129 and returns without changing height. A neighbor at 127 above target -1 prevents digging. A neighbor at -128 beside target 3 permits digging, as the source does. These differences fit i16; the admitted target still fits i8 before storage. This fixes the actual overflow path without imposing a new terrain-value rule.

The existing original-source recorder primes correctly sized Int8 layers and runs actual blast damage through `dent`, touch and cell mutation. Four new traces cover minimum height, both extreme neighbor differences and valid level 4 lowering to 3. All 44 previous traces remain byte-equivalent as parsed cases. Independent copied binaries pass all five focused tests in each profile. Debug exercises the former checked Int8 subtraction overflow; release exercises the former wrapping and wrong-slope outcomes. ## World administrative elimination (2026-10-13)

The explicit `apply_world_capture_income_and_elimination` phase accepts two ordered lists: the captured tick list for upkeep and the complete current unit collection for removal and collapse queries. It preserves the original outer player loop, including already-out commanders, and removes each selected object before wrecking that building. A prior wreck can evict a later selected garrison, so its removal snapshot is captured at that later deletion. Deleted squads, vehicles and aircraft produce no ordinary death, tally, bounty or refund callback. Matching building depots clear through the actual wreck path. The earlier capture-only entry point still refuses armies that need removal.

Only a phase with actual removals creates an ephemeral World, Environment, maintained-grid and Combat draft. Shared environment seed and next-ID state are staged with it. Original geography, initial terrain and rules remain the single immutable owners; publication copies prepared terrain values into the original allocations before installing the draft. Journal prefixes, terrain revisions and recipient pending cursors must still match. The synchronous caller owns the entire source exclusively throughout preparation; the guard does not claim to detect independent health or material writes. Every terrain-array shape is checked before staged wreck code can index it. This bounded copy makes late wreck, finish, story or cache errors leave all canonical owners intact. It is not the ordinary per-tick execution path, and no performance result has been measured.

Finish preparation receives complete post-wreck survivor facts plus ordered removed IDs. Recipient retention prepares each final removed object's finite observation fields while its canonical ID still exists; success updates only matching cached objects without refreshing visibility or route membership. Commands and pending path requests, production queues, rally/recruitment rows, reserves, ability/recovery rows and paid work are removed through their owners. The transition harness restores explicit flat terrain and current unit facts after the actual constructor, then registers real sections through the original function. It does not claim matched bootstrap random streams or leave unrelated starter footprints in its physics scene. Six original constructor-based transitions record player-first removal order, signed health, allied survival, actual garrison eviction before a later deletion, depot cleanup and real debris overflow at 97 registered sections. Native exact effects/rows and late-error rollback tests now pass. The 46 affected debug tests passed at runtime-identical `2169e7b`; subsequent changes are the checked observer test projection repair and formatter output. A separate witness imports an already source-compared ordinary flight into World, preserving its payload and retained XP through administrative removal; no World flight execution is enabled. ## Optimized CI tests with debug checks (2026-10-13)

The separate `ci-test` Cargo profile inherits the test profile, uses optimization level 2 and explicitly keeps debug assertions and integer overflow checks. It disables debug symbols and incremental output to keep the one-shot CI build bounded. Cargo documents that optimization level and diagnostic checks are independent settings. Higher optimization may shorten test execution while extending compilation; the whole command duration must be measured rather than inferred. See https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/profiles.html.

Only the profile selected by the existing whole-workspace test step changes. The test sources, inputs, deterministic math, default developer profile, release artifact profile, three operating systems and all other CI gates are unchanged. The configuration is an isolated child of the frozen all-original PR #193 head `a8f0b00`; it does not change that original attempt. The child can advance the same PR after verification, with fresh checks for its exact head. No game performance claim follows from test duration.


### Measured Mac verification

At compiled configuration `5777e16`, the two-job Mac run completed the same whole workspace suite: 362 passed, zero failed or ignored, with all 362 executed test names matching the completed `a8f0b00` release suite. The optimized test command took 491.76 seconds, including 4 minutes 16 seconds of new-profile compilation. The same 86-test core suite took 37.52 seconds, compared with 357.84 seconds in the unoptimized `a8f0b00` Mac proof. These are test-command durations on this Mac, not a prediction for hosted runners. Workspace package outputs were cleaned before the run; dependency caches remained.

Cargo artifact metadata for all 90 test executables confirms optimization level 2, zero debug symbols, debug assertions enabled and overflow checks enabled. Formatting, strict workspace/all-target Clippy and the unchanged locked release FFI build also exited zero. Exact commands, times, source and log hashes are preserved in `/private/tmp/ww2-ci-profile-5777e16-proof/manifest.json`; the matching test-name audit is `semantics-audit.json` and the actual profile metadata is `test-profiles.json`. Production sources, test bodies, fixtures, reference pin and approved ABI 2 header are byte-identical to `a8f0b00`. The Linux C gate remains in the unchanged workflow; this Mac measurement does not substitute for its fresh hosted result.
